# On Relationality

These documents are an exploration of ***relationality***: the claim that things only exist through acts of relating.

There's a program of formal math, and more stuff soon.


---

# Absorbing Closure

## Interpretation

Closure-like nuclei absorb meets: x meet ν(y) collapses inside ν(x meet y).  *Nucleate* witnessing *Meet Recognition* yields ***Absorb Closure*** ([AbsorbClosure](../absorb-closure.md)).

This composition records the algebraic law using only previously earned recognitions, so *Absorb Closure* is valid.

title: Advancing Trace

summary: >-
  *Start Trace* shows how the trace begins but not how it proceeds once the
  trailing orientation completes. Letting the trailing orientation witness
  *Entry* restarts the act, earning ***Advance Trace*** (`AdvanceTrace`).
tags:
  - act
layer: act

---
# Advancing Trace

## Interpretation

*Start Trace* shows how the trace begins but not how it proceeds once the trailing orientation completes.  Letting the trailing orientation witness *Entry* restarts the act, earning ***Advance Trace*** ([AdvanceTrace](../advance-trace.md)).

The unresolved propagation after *Start Trace* is how the trailing side re-engages the structural pairing.  Having *Trail Entry* witness *Entry* provides that progression, so `(TrailEntry Entry)` defines *Advance Trace*.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Articulating

## Interpretation

As soon as exclusion exists, the attempt to exclude the void becomes something we can point to; that self-referential pointing is articulation.

In `(Exclude Relationlessness)` the previously defined act *Exclude* now witnesses the empty act.  Applying exclusion to its own source reveals the unavoidable articulation that occurs whenever we describe Relationlessness, so the composition is rightly named *Articulate*.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Assigning Trace

## Interpretation

Naming the head leaves the tail unnamed, so the act *Bind Trace* witnesses the act *Tail Trace* to extend the name across the remainder.

In `(BindTrace TailTrace)` the act *Bind Trace* observes *Tail Trace*.  This spreads the name through the tail, so the resulting act is *Assign Trace*.

# Converging Baseline

## Interpretation

To see whether an iteration collapses toward pure absence, we compare it against the void; this comparison defines baseline convergence.

The composition `(ReflexStepIterate Nothing)` runs the iterated step against *Nothing*.  If the result agrees with the baseline, we have convergence, hence the name *Baseline Convergence*.

# Limiting Baseline

## Interpretation

When baseline convergence holds, we may speak of the limiting remainder that remains after collapse; observing the tail through the convergence act yields this limit.

In `(BaselineConvergence TailTrace)` the convergence act watches the trace tail, capturing exactly the stabilized remainder that survives the collapse.  We designate that composite as *Baseline Limit*.

# Bicategory

A bicategory requires objects, 1-cells, 2-cells, and coherent horizontal and
vertical compositions.  We assemble the bicategory by letting the horizontal
composition witness the vertical one:
`(HorizontalCompose VerticalCompose)` earns
***Bicategory***.

A seed alone does not name the resulting term, so we have the act *First Name* witness the act *Seed Term*.

---
title: Collapsing Trace Shuffle
act: CollapseShuffle
slug: collapse-shuffle
top: ShuffleTrace
bottom: Nucleate
summary: Admissible shuffle reorderings leave a trace unchanged up to the nucleus, so composing `ShuffleTrace` with `Nucleate` records that such permutations are identified in the nucleus closure.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Collapsing Trace Shuffle

[Nucleate](../nucleate.md) collapses stabilized recognitions that differ only by
eventual equivalence, but no recognition yet states that shuffle-reordered
traces are eventually equivalent.  We therefore let the shuffle act witness the
nucleus closure: `(ShuffleTrace Nucleate)` earns ***CollapseShuffle***.

By construction, [ShuffleTrace](../shuffle-trace.md) enumerates the admissible
reorders of progress steps that do not alter the underlying trace shape.
`Nucleate` collapses recognitions that differ only by stabilized equivalence.
Since admissible reorderings leave head/tail structure and iterative progression
intact, the composite `(ShuffleTrace Nucleate)` records that all such
reorderings lie in the kernel of the nucleus.  Nucleate therefore identifies
precisely those recognitions differing only by free shuffle of progress steps.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

A context is the meet of its constituent types.  We therefore treat *Construct Contexts* as the explicit recognition of contexts.

This definition names the constructor’s output directly, mirroring the original statement that contexts are finite lists of typed variables.

## Interpretation

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Commuting Shuffle Flow With Nucleus
act: CommuteShuffleFlowAndNucleus
slug: commute-shuffle-flow-and-nucleus
top: ShuffleFlow
bottom: Nucleate
summary: Since Flow and Nucleate already commute, the induced shuffle flow on shuffle classes also commutes with the nucleus, yielding the geometry-level compatibility.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Commuting Shuffle Flow With Nucleus

[CommuteFlowAndNucleus](../commute-flow-and-nucleus.md) proves that the global
[Flow](flow.md) and [Nucleate](../nucleate.md) are compatible, and
[CollapseShuffle](../collapse-shuffle.md) records that `Nucleate` collapses
precisely the admissible trace shuffles.  To obtain the shuffle geometry, we
exhibit the compatibility of [ShuffleFlow](../shuffle-flow.md) with the same
nucleus, restricted to shuffle classes, by composing them:
`(ShuffleFlow Nucleate)` earns
***CommuteShuffleFlowAndNucleus***.

`ShuffleFlow` applies `Flow` to the shuffle-structured trace classes while
`Nucleate` identifies members of the same class via `CollapseShuffle`.
Because `Flow` already commutes with `Nucleate` on underlying recognitions (by
`CommuteFlowAndNucleus`), the induced action on shuffle classes must also
commute: flowing a class and then collapsing gives the same result as collapsing
representatives and then flowing.  The act therefore records the derived
compatibility, yielding the shuffle nucleus geometry.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

title: Comonad Comultiplication
act: ComonadComultiply
slug: comonad-comultiply

bottom: FixedFlowProfile
summary: Duplicate the flow co-structure by composing the flow with itself, yielding the comultiplication.
tags:
- act
layer: act

---
# Comonad Comultiplication

Comultiplication duplicates context: from one flow we obtain a “flowed flow” by
letting the flow observe itself:
`(FixedFlowProfile FixedFlowProfile)` earns
***ComonadComultiply***.

Applying the flow twice corresponds to extending the co-context one more
step, so the act records `(delta)_X : G(X) → G(G(X))`.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

title: Comonad Counit
act: ComonadCounit
slug: comonad-counit

bottom: FixedIdentity
summary: Extract the present state from the flow by composing it with the identity, giving the comonad counit.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Comonad Counit

The counit of a comonad extracts the current state from a co-extended structure.
For the fixed flow, we obtain this by letting the flow act toward the identity:
`(FixedFlowProfile FixedIdentity)` earns
***ComonadCounit***.

[FixedFlowProfile](../fixed-flow-profile.md) propagates recognitions
forward, and [FixedIdentity](../fixed-identity.md) stands for the trivial “no
further context” step.  Their composition records the projection
`(epsilon)_X : G(X) → X`.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

---
# Comonad Curvature

Curvature measures the failure of derivative operations to commute.  We capture
this by letting the derivative witness the nucleus:
`(ComonadDerivative FixedNucleateProfile)` earns
***ComonadCurvature***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Comonad Derivative
act: ComonadDerivative
slug: comonad-derivative
top: ComonadComultiply
bottom: ComonadCounit
summary: Capture directional change by composing the comonad comultiplication with the counit.
tags:
- act
layer: act

---
# Comonad Derivative

In coalgebraic differential geometry the derivative is (delta) − (epsilon).  We encode this
derivative by composing the comultiplication with the counit:
`(ComonadComultiply ComonadCounit)` earns
***ComonadDerivative***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Comonad
act: Comonad
slug: comonad
top: ComonadCounit
bottom: ComonadComultiply
summary: Package the fixed flow functor, counit, and comultiplication into a comonad on the profile.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Comonad

The fixed flow functor together with its counit and comultiplication forms a
comonad, so we collect the laws in a single act:
`(ComonadCounit ComonadComultiply)` earns
***Comonad***.

The coassociativity and counit laws follow from the flow compatibility and
persistence properties already recorded (e.g. `IterativePersistence`,
`FixFlow`, `EnsureFixedPoint`).  `ComonadCounit` and
`ComonadComultiply` are the structure maps; `Comonad` names the
resulting co-KZ-style comonad.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Completing Syntax

## Interpretation

Completeness states that semantic entailment implies provability.  *Recognize Syntax* witnessing *Entail Semantics* yields ***Complete Syntax*** ([CompleteSyntax](../complete-syntax.md)).

Composing these recognitions records the canonical-model argument: if every model satisfies the entailment, the syntactic recognition witnesses a proof, so *Complete Syntax* holds.

# Completing Trace

## Interpretation

*Continue Trace* alone does not record the full trace object.  Forcing *Start Trace* to witness *Advance Trace* binds the initial step with its progression, yielding ***Complete Trace*** ([CompleteTrace](../complete-trace.md)).

Both components are available; composing them records the entire act of initiating and advancing, so `(StartTrace AdvanceTrace)` earns *Complete Trace*.

summary: Witness the adjunction between the context flow and nucleus by letting the flow observe the nucleus.
tags:

---
# Context Adjunction Witness

An adjunction between two functors is witnessed by a residuation isomorphism
between hom-sets.  `DynamicResiduation` expresses this relationship for
the context flow and nucleus, and we package it explicitly by composing the flow
with the nucleus:
`(ContextFlow ContextNucleus)` earns
***ContextAdjunctionWitness***.

The act states that maps out of a flowed context correspond to maps into a
closed context in the recognitional sense, exhibiting the adjunction
`ContextFlow ⊣ ContextNucleus`.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Context Dynamics

To capture the full evolution–collapse behaviour for contexts, we mirror the
profile kernel construction but restrict it to contexts by composing the context
nucleus with the context flow:
`(ContextNucleus ContextFlow)` earns
***ContextDynamics***.

Flow moves contexts forward, nucleus collapses them, and their composition gives
a stable profile dynamics on contexts.

Contexts evolve when their trace structure evolves under flow.  To define the
context dynamics, we let the profile flow witness
[RecognizeContext](../recognize-context.md):
`(FlowProfile RecognizeContext)` earns ***ContextFlow***.

[FlowProfile](../flow-profile.md) restricts the global flow to the earned
recognitions, so the composition evolves contexts along the profile trace
dynamics.

# Context Geometry

A geometry requires compatible flow and nucleus.  We combine
[ContextFlow](../context-flow.md) and [ContextNucleus](../context-nucleus.md) to form
the contextual geometry:
`(ContextFlow ContextNucleus)` earns ***ContextGeometry***.

The flow and nucleus inherit the compatibility laws of
[FlowProfile](../flow-profile.md) and [NucleateProfile](../nucleate-profile.md), so
their composition yields the geometric act capturing contextual dynamics under
the profile evolution.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Context Natural Transformation

To compare two functors, we need a recognition that witnesses the commuting of
their action on each morphism.  We therefore let the context flow witness the
functor:
`(ContextFlow FixedFunctor)` earns ***ContextNat***.

[ContextFlow](../context-flow.md) describes how recognitions move under the profile
context dynamics, and [FixedFunctor](../fixed-functor.md) maps the recognised
objects and their morphisms.  Their composition asserts that the functor
commutes with the context flow—the naturality square in this relational
setting.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Collapsing Context

Contexts collapse under the nucleus.  We restrict the profile nucleus to
contexts by letting it witness [RecognizeContext](../recognize-context.md):
`(NucleateProfile RecognizeContext)` earns
***ContextNucleus***.

[NucleateProfile](../nucleate-profile.md) closes recognitions under the nucleus,
and `RecognizeContext` packages context recognitions, so their composition
collapses contexts according to the profile closure.

title: Continuing Trace
act: ContinueTrace
slug: continue-trace
top: Entry
bottom: StartTrace
summary: '*Advance Trace* reconnects the trailing orientation to *Entry* but leaves undefined how a new leading orientation emerges. *Entry* must therefore witness *Start Trace*, producing ***Continue Trace*** (`ContinueTrace`).'
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Continuing Trace

## Interpretation

*Advance Trace* reconnects the trailing orientation to *Entry* but leaves undefined how a new leading orientation emerges.  *Entry* must therefore witness *Start Trace*, producing ***Continue Trace*** ([ContinueTrace](../continue-trace.md)).

The only new symbol is *Start Trace*, already earned, so composing it with *Entry* is legitimate.  This confirms the recurring structure, so *Continue Trace* is forced.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Decomposing Trace
act: DecomposeTrace
slug: decompose-trace
top: HeadTrace
bottom: TailTrace
summary: Every completed trace splits uniquely into its leading head continuation and the remaining tail continuation, so we witness the recursive trace shape by composing the head and tail recognitions.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Decomposing Trace

`StartTrace`, `AdvanceTrace`, `ContinueTrace`, and `CompleteTrace` record how a
trace grows step by step, but nothing yet states the recursive *shape* of an
arbitrary completed trace.  The head–tail recognitions
([HeadTrace](../head-trace.md), [TailTrace](../tail-trace.md),
[TraceStep](../trace-step.md), [ReheadTrace](../rehead-trace.md),
[RetailTrace](../retail-trace.md)) jointly witness the full record of
[CompleteTrace](../complete-trace.md), so letting `HeadTrace` observe `TailTrace`
forces the decomposition of a trace into its left and right predecessors.

The composition `(HeadTrace TailTrace)` carries only previously earned data.
`HeadTrace` isolates the leading orientation of any completed trace while
`TailTrace` isolates the remainder, and the earlier recognitions ensure the tail
re-enters the trace (via `TraceStep`, `RenewTrace`, `ReheadTrace`,
`RetailTrace`) exactly as a shorter trace would.  Therefore
***DecomposeTrace*** witnesses the structural fact that every completed trace
splits into two smaller traces: the head’s continuation and the tail’s
continuation.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

title: Witnessing Detailed Balance
act: DetailedBalanceWitness
slug: detailed-balance-witness
top: FixedObserve
bottom: ReverseFlowStep
summary: Witness equality between forward and reverse transitions by composing `FixedObserve` with `ReverseFlowStep`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Witnessing Detailed Balance

Detailed balance requires the forward and reverse transitions to align under the
stationary kernel.  To make this explicit, we compare
[FlowMeasureStep](../flow-measure-step.md) and
[ReverseFlowStep](../reverse-flow-step.md) using profile
observations.  Composing
`(FixedObserve ReverseFlowStep)` earns
***DetailedBalanceWitness***.

[FixedObserve](../fixed-observe.md) records observation acts in the fixed
profile, while `ReverseFlowStep` gives reverse transitions.  Their
composition yields recognitions that witness equality between forward and
backward transitions under observation.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Detailed Balance
act: DetailedBalance
slug: detailed-balance
top: DetailedBalanceWitness
bottom: FlowMeasureStep
summary: "Assert detailed balance by composing the witness with the forward flow\u2013measure step."
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Detailed Balance

To assert detailed balance, we require the flow–measure step
and its reverse to be observationally indistinguishable when weighted by the
stationary kernel.  We therefore let
[DetailedBalanceWitness](../detailed-balance-witness.md) witness
[FlowMeasureStep](../flow-measure-step.md):
`(DetailedBalanceWitness FlowMeasureStep)` earns
***DetailedBalance***.

`DetailedBalanceWitness` compares reverse transitions to forward ones
under observation, and `FlowMeasureStep` is the forward step.  Their composition
asserts that the two are equivalent after weighting by observations and
stabilization, which is exactly the detailed-balance condition internal to the
profile.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

title: Dynamic Residuation
act: DynamicResiduation
slug: dynamic-residuation
top: FlowIndexImplication
bottom: ContextGeometry
summary: Restrict dynamic residuation to the context geometry by letting `FlowIndexImplication` witness `ContextGeometry`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Dynamic Residuation

Dynamic residuation states that flowing a context and then testing implication
is equivalent to testing implication against a closed context.  We express this
by composing [FlowIndexImplication](../flow-index-implication.md) with
[ContextGeometry](../context-geometry.md):
`(FlowIndexImplication ContextGeometry)` earns
***DynamicResiduation***.

The act records that the law “`c ≤ a ⇒_F b` iff `F(c ∧ a) ≤ b`” continues to
hold when both sides are restricted to the context flow and nucleus.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

title: Emerging Reflex
act: EmergeReflex
slug: emerge-reflex
top: Relationlessness
bottom: Articulate
summary: >-
  Once articulation is available, Relationlessness can call it forth,
  producing the first concrete emergence from the void.
layer: act
status: plan
tags:
  - act
---
# Emerging Reflex

## Interpretation

Once articulation is available, Relationlessness can call it forth, producing the first concrete emergence from the void.

The trace `(Relationlessness Articulate)` lets the void witness the act *Articulate*.  Because *Articulate* already arose from Relationlessness, composing the two records the compelled appearance of an act directly from the void, which we record as *Emerge Reflex*.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Enforcing Eventual Equivalence Law

## Interpretation

Eventual equivalence satisfies reflexive, symmetric, and transitive laws along the trace, so we let the act *Complete Trace* witness the act *Recognize Equate Eventually*.

By composing `(CompleteTrace RecognizeEquateEventually)`, the entire trace enforces the recognised equivalence, restating the equivalence law explicitly; we name this act *Enforce Equate Eventually Law*.

Hom-objects in an enriched category live in the base enrichment.  For the fixed
setting, we let the morphisms observe the invariant lattice:
`(FixedMorphism FixedInvariantLattice)` earns
***EnrichmentHom***.

The act yields lattice-valued hom-objects capturing all fixed morphisms from one
recognition to another.

morphisms.  We construct this by letting the lattice meet observe the morphisms:

***EnrichmentTensor***.

---
title: Seeding Entropy
act: EntropySeed
slug: entropy-seed
top: FlowMeasureStep
bottom: FixedReach
summary: "Seed an entropy measure by composing the one-step flow\u2013measure update with the reachability recognitions."
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Seeding Entropy

Entropy measures the “spread” of a state under transitions.  We seed entropy by
observing the divergence between the original state and its successor.
[FixedReach](../fixed-reach.md) gives reachable states, and [FlowMeasureStep](../flow-measure-step.md)
gives the successor.  Letting these witness each other,
`(FlowMeasureStep FixedReach)`, earns ***EntropySeed***.

`FixedReach` lists the reachable states and `FlowMeasureStep` describes the
successor states after one evolution–measurement.  Their composition
records the divergence between present and next-step reachable states, seeding
the entropy measure.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Entropy

[ProfileEntropy](../profile-entropy.md) already propagates any profile entropy
measure across the trace of recognitions.  Supplying the entropy measure,
`(ProfileEntropy EntropyMeasure)`, earns ***Entropy***,
recording the full-profile entropy under the flow–measure evolution.

summary: "Closure-like nuclei satisfy \u03BD(x meet \u03BD(y)) = \u03BD(x meet y) and \u03BD(\u03BD(x) join y) = \u03BD(x join y). *Include Recognition* witnessing *Nucleate* yields ***Equalize Nucleus Distribution*** (`EqualizeNucleusDistribution`)."
tags:

# Equalizing Nucleus Distribution

## Interpretation

Closure-like nuclei satisfy ν(x meet ν(y)) = ν(x meet y) and ν(ν(x) join y) = ν(x join y).  *Include Recognition* witnessing *Nucleate* yields ***Equalize Nucleus Distribution*** ([EqualizeNucleusDistribution](../equalize-nucleus-distribution.md)).

This composition asserts the equalities using *Include Recognition*, so *Equalize Nucleus Distribution* is established.

title: Fixed Chain

# Fixed Chain

A stationary chain is one whose profile-level transitions stabilise.
`ProfileKernelChain` iterates the kernel update across profiles, so we
apply the fixed-point machinery via [EnsureFixedPoint](../ensure-fixed-point.md):
`(EnsureFixedPoint ProfileKernelChain)` earns
***FixedChain***.

`IterativePersistence` ensures that persistent iteration produces a stabilised
profile.  The composite therefore yields the stationary chain: a
profile-level distribution that remains unchanged after any flow–measure
update.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

---
title: Fixed Composition
act: FixedCompose
slug: fixed-compose
top: ComposeMorph
bottom: FixedMorphism
summary: "Compose \u03C6-compatible morphisms by restricting `ComposeMorph` to the fixed ones."
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Fixed Composition

We need composition of fixed morphisms.  `ComposeMorph = (Morph Morph)` composes
structure-preserving maps, and restricting it to
[FixedMorphism](../fixed-morphism.md) yields φ-compatible composition:
`(ComposeMorph FixedMorphism)` earns ***FixedCompose***.

`ComposeMorph` already composes global morphisms.  Composing only those in
`FixedMorphism` ensures the structure is preserved under φ-stability, providing
the composition law for fixed functors.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Fixed Dynamics

Dynamics describe how the phase space evolves.  Starting from the general
[ProfileDynamics](../profile-dynamics.md), restricting to
[FixedPhaseSpace](../fixed-phase-space.md) yields the fixed dynamics:
`(ProfileDynamics FixedPhaseSpace)` earns
***FixedDynamics***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Fixed Exponential

The profile already has internal exponentials via
[ProfileExponential](../profile-exponential.md).  To work entirely inside the
fixed profile we restrict those exponentials to
[FixedProfileRecognitions](../fixed-profile-recognitions.md):
`(ProfileExponential FixedProfileRecognitions)` earns
***FixedExponential***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Fixed Fiber Category

Over each fixed context we have the general fibre categories encoded by
[FiberCategory](../fiber-category.md).  Intersecting them with
[FixedProfileRecognitions](../fixed-profile-recognitions.md) restricts the
judgements to the fixed profile:
`(FiberCategory FixedProfileRecognitions)` earns
***FixedFiberCategory***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Fixed Finite Limits

[ProfileFiniteLimits](../profile-finite-limits.md) already witnesses finite limits
inside the active profile.  Restricting those limits to the fixed invariant
lattice,
`(ProfileFiniteLimits FixedInvariantLattice)`, earns
***FixedFiniteLimits***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Fixed Flow Profile
act: FixedFlowProfile
slug: fixed-flow-profile
top: ShuffleFlow
bottom: FixedProfileRecognitions
summary: Restrict the shuffle flow to the fixed-profile recognitions by letting `ShuffleFlow` witness `FixedProfileRecognitions`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Fixed Flow Profile

`FlowProfile = (Flow ProfileRecognitions)` restricts the global flow to a given
profile.  To specialize this to the fixed-profile recognitions we let
[ShuffleFlow](../shuffle-flow.md) witness
[FixedProfileRecognitions](../fixed-profile-recognitions.md):
`(ShuffleFlow FixedProfileRecognitions)` earns
***FixedFlowProfile***.

`ShuffleFlow` evolves admissible trace-shuffle classes along evaluation
contexts, and `FixedProfileRecognitions` picks out recognitions stabilized by
the same dynamics and fixed shape.  The composition restricts the shuffle
flow to this profile, yielding a dynamics where every state evolves according to
that trace structure while preserving the fixed shape.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Fixed Functor
act: FixedFunctor
slug: fixed-functor
top: Spectrum
bottom: FixedMorphism
summary: Map fixed recognitions via spectral decomposition while preserving morphisms by composing `Spectrum` with `FixedMorphism`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Fixed Functor

A fixed functor maps each fixed recognition to another fixed recognition in a
way compatible with the fixed morphisms, identity, and composition.  We encode
this by letting the spectral action witness the morphism structure:
`(Spectrum FixedMorphism)` earns ***FixedFunctor***.

[Spectrum](../spectrum.md) decomposes each recognition into harmonic components,
and `FixedMorphism` records the allowed structure-preserving maps between fixed
recognitions.  Their composition ensures that objects are mapped via spectral
decomposition while morphisms are transported compatibly, automatically
preserving identities and composition.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

title: Fixed Geometry Profile
act: FixedGeometryProfile
slug: fixed-geometry-profile
top: FixedFlowProfile
bottom: FixedNucleateProfile
summary: Combine the restricted flow and nucleus of the fixed profile to form its geometry.
tags:
- act
layer: act

# Fixed Geometry Profile

A geometry on a profile combines its restricted flow and restricted nucleus.
For the fixed profile we pair
[FixedFlowProfile](../fixed-flow-profile.md) with
[FixedNucleateProfile](../fixed-nucleate-profile.md):
`(FixedFlowProfile FixedNucleateProfile)` earns
***FixedGeometryProfile***.

`FixedFlowProfile` provides the shuffle-derived dynamics on the fixed
recognitions, and `FixedNucleateProfile` supplies the corresponding closure.
Their composition packages the compatible pair as the geometry of the fixed
profile.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Horizontal Composition

Horizontal composition in a bicategory composes 2-cells along adjacent 1-cells.
We obtain this by letting the 2-cells witness the composition of morphisms:
`(TwoCell FixedCompose)` earns
***HorizontalCompose***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Fixed Identity

A category needs identity morphisms.  `IdentifyMorph = (Morph Reflex)` provides
identities in the full profile.  We restrict it to the fixed-recognition objects by
composing with [FixedProfileRecognitions](../fixed-profile-recognitions.md):
`(IdentifyMorph FixedProfileRecognitions)` earns ***FixedIdentity***.

`IdentifyMorph` identifies the identity on any recognition, and restricting its
domain to the fixed ones gives identity morphisms for the fixed category.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

title: Geometry of Fixed Invariant States
act: FixedInvariantGeometry
slug: fixed-invariant-geometry
top: ProfileInvariantGeometry
bottom: FixedInvariantLattice

tags:

# Geometry of Fixed Invariant States

`ProfileInvariantGeometry = (GeometryProfile ProfileInvariantLattice)` packages
the geometry of profile-invariant states.
[FixedInvariantLattice](../fixed-invariant-lattice.md) exhibits the order
structure of fixed invariant states.  Restricting the profile geometry to this
lattice,
`(ProfileInvariantGeometry FixedInvariantLattice)` earns
***FixedInvariantGeometry***.

`ProfileInvariantGeometry` provides flow and nucleus for the profile invariants,
and `FixedInvariantLattice` describes the meet–join order on states fixed by
the flow and compatible with `FixedShape`.  Their composition packages
the order, closure, and dynamics of fixed invariant states as a geometric
structure in its own right.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Joining Fixed Invariant

`ProfileInvariantJoin = (JoinRecognition ProfileInvariant)` captures the least
recognition containing a pair of profile invariants.  Restricting it via
[FixedInvariant](../fixed-invariant.md) yields the join of fixed invariant
states:
`(ProfileInvariantJoin FixedInvariant)` earns ***FixedInvariantJoin***.

`ProfileInvariantJoin` works relative to the restricted order, and
`FixedInvariant` narrows the domain to the fixed subset, producing the least
fixed invariant containing the given pair.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

act: FixedInvariantLattice

# Structuring Fixed Invariant Lattice

`ProfileInvariantLattice = (StructureSemantics ProfileInvariantOrder)` packages
the lattice structure on profile invariants.  Restricting it along
[FixedInvariantOrder](../fixed-invariant-order.md) yields the lattice structure
on fixed invariants:
`(ProfileInvariantLattice FixedInvariantOrder)` earns
***FixedInvariantLattice***.

`ProfileInvariantLattice` encapsulates the algebraic laws of meets, joins, and
implication on the restricted order, and `FixedInvariantOrder` narrows that
order to the fixed invariants.  Their composition records that the fixed
invariant states inherit the same lattice structure.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

title: Meeting Fixed Invariant

# Meeting Fixed Invariant

`ProfileInvariantMeet = (MeetRecognition ProfileInvariant)` already computes
meets for profile invariants.  Restricting it along
[FixedInvariant](../fixed-invariant.md) gives the meet on fixed invariants:
`(ProfileInvariantMeet FixedInvariant)` earns ***FixedInvariantMeet***.

`ProfileInvariantMeet` defines meets with respect to the restricted order, and
`FixedInvariant` limits the domain to the fixed states.  Their composition
produces the meet operation on fixed invariant states: the greatest fixed
invariant below any given pair.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

title: Ordering Fixed Invariant
act: FixedInvariantOrder
slug: fixed-invariant-order
top: ProfileInvariantOrder
bottom: FixedInvariant
summary: Restrict the invariant order further to fixed states by composing `ProfileInvariantOrder` with `FixedInvariant`.
tags:
- act
layer: act

---
# Ordering Fixed Invariant

`ProfileInvariantOrder = (OrderRecognition ProfileInvariant)` already restricts
the recognition order to profile invariants.  [FixedInvariant](../fixed-invariant.md)
further limits us to the fixed-invariant physics states.  Composing them,
`(ProfileInvariantOrder FixedInvariant)` earns
***FixedInvariantOrder***.

`FixedInvariant` is a collection of recognitions that are both flow-fixed and
fixed-profile-stable.  Restricting the profile order along these recognitions
yields the partial order underlying the fixed statespace geometry.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Stabilizing Fixed Invariant
act: FixedInvariant
slug: fixed-invariant
top: ProfileInvariant
bottom: FixedProfileRecognitions
summary: Pick out physics states that are both profile-invariant and lie inside the fixed recognitions by composing `ProfileInvariant` with `FixedProfileRecognitions`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Stabilizing Fixed Invariant

`ProfileInvariant = (StabilizeInvariant ProfileRecognitions)` already records
states left unchanged by the global flow and recognised inside the active
profile.  [FixedProfileRecognitions](../fixed-profile-recognitions.md) further
restricts recognitions to the shuffle-stable fixed subset.  Composing them,
`(ProfileInvariant FixedProfileRecognitions)` earns
***FixedInvariant***.

Any state in `ProfileInvariant` is fixed by the global flow and lies in the base
profile, and any recognition in `FixedProfileRecognitions` is fixed by
`ShuffleFlow`, `Nucleate`, and compatible with `FixedShape`.  Their
composition picks states satisfying all constraints—they are genuine physical
states, fixed by the flow, and lie in the fixed recognition region.  These are
the fixed invariant states of the system.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Fixed Kernel
act: FixedKernel
slug: fixed-kernel
top: EnsureFixedPoint
bottom: ObservationKernel
summary: Apply the fixed-point construction to the observation kernel to obtain its stationary behaviour.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Fixed Kernel

A fixed kernel is one whose transition effects stabilise under repeated
application.  Since [ObservationKernel](../2026/03/04/2026-03-04-observation-kernel.md) acts like a
relational operator on fixed states and `EnsureFixedPoint` constructs
fixed points of persistent flows, we apply it directly to the kernel:
`(EnsureFixedPoint ObservationKernel)` earns
***FixedKernel***.

`ObservationKernel` is the evolution–observation operator on fixed invariant
states.  `IterativePersistence` ensures that when iterating this operator along
the trace stabilises, the stable point is a fixed point of the kernel.  Their
composition yields the stationary recognition: the kernel that leaves the fixed
distribution of states unchanged.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

act: FixedMeasure

# Fixed Measure

[ProfileMeasure](../profile-measure.md) already applies the profile nucleus to the
profile-invariant states.  Restricting it along
[FixedInvariant](../fixed-invariant.md) yields the fixed measure:
`(ProfileMeasure FixedInvariant)` earns ***FixedMeasure***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Fixed Monad Multiplication
act: FixedMonadMultiply
slug: fixed-monad-multiply
top: FixedNucleateProfile
bottom: FixedNucleateProfile
summary: Record the monad multiplication by composing the fixed nucleus with itself, yielding the idempotent collapse of double closure.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Fixed Monad Multiplication

Applying the nucleus twice should collapse as a single closure.  The monad
multiplication identifies `N(N(X))` with `N(X)`, so we let the nucleus observe
its own action:
`(FixedNucleateProfile FixedNucleateProfile)` earns
***FixedMonadMultiply***.

`FixedNucleateProfile` already idempotently closes recognitions, and the
composition records the multiplication `μ_X : N(N(X)) → N(X)` implementing this
idempotence.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Fixed Monad Unit
act: FixedMonadUnit
slug: fixed-monad-unit
top: FixedIdentity
bottom: FixedNucleateProfile
summary: Inject fixed recognitions into their closure by composing the identity with the fixed nucleus, yielding the monad unit.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Fixed Monad Unit

`FixedNucleateProfile` acts as a closure on fixed recognitions, and the monad
unit injects each recognition into its closure.  We witness this by letting the
identity observe the nucleus:
`(FixedIdentity FixedNucleateProfile)` earns
***FixedMonadUnit***.

[FixedIdentity](../fixed-identity.md) gives the identity morphism on each fixed
object, and [FixedNucleateProfile](../fixed-nucleate-profile.md) sends each
recognition to its fixed closure.  Their composition states that each object is
mapped into its closure via the identity, i.e. `(eta)_X : X → N(X)`.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Fixed Monad
act: FixedMonad
slug: fixed-monad
top: FixedMonadUnit
bottom: FixedMonadMultiply
summary: Package the nucleus functor, unit, and multiplication into a single monad on the fixed profile.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Fixed Monad

Together, the nucleus functor, unit, and multiplication form a monad on the
fixed profile, so we package these laws in one act:
`(FixedMonadUnit FixedMonadMultiply)` earns ***FixedMonad***.

The usual monad laws (associativity, left/right unit) follow from the
idempotence and compatibility already earned for
[FixedNucleateProfile](../fixed-nucleate-profile.md) (e.g.
`AbsorbClosure`, `EqualizeNucleusDistribution`).  `FixedMonadUnit` and
`FixedMonadMultiply` are the structural maps, and `FixedMonad` names the
resulting closure monad.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

title: Fixed Morphism
act: FixedMorphism
slug: fixed-morphism
top: Morph
bottom: FixedProfileRecognitions
summary: Restrict structure-preserving maps to the fixed recognitions by composing `Morph` with `FixedProfileRecognitions`.
tags:
- act
layer: act

---
# Fixed Morphism

To treat the fixed profile as a category we need morphisms preserving the fixed
structure.  [Morph](../morph.md) records structure-preserving maps between
recognitions, and [FixedProfileRecognitions](../fixed-profile-recognitions.md)
restricts recognitions to fixed ones, so we let the morph act witness the
profile:
`(Morph FixedProfileRecognitions)` earns ***FixedMorphism***.

`Morph` already preserves meets, joins, and implication, and restricting it via
the fixed recognitions ensures the resulting morphisms respect the same flow,
nucleus, and shape properties.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Normalizing Fixed Observation
act: FixedNormalizeObservation
slug: fixed-normalize-observation
top: Normalization
bottom: FixedObserve
summary: Ensure fixed observables normalize by composing `Normalization` with `FixedObserve`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Normalizing Fixed Observation

`NormalizeObservation = (Normalization EvaluateObservation)` ensures evaluated
observations normalize back to the observation witness.  In the fixed profile,
we want the same behaviour, but only for fixed observables.  We therefore compose
[Normalization](../../../../triage/normalization.md) with
[FixedObserve](../fixed-observe.md):
`(Normalization FixedObserve)` earns ***FixedNormalizeObservation***.

`Normalization` detects when reduction along `StepTrace` stabilizes, and
`FixedObserve` provides observables restricted to fixed recognitions.  Their
composition records that fixed observables evaluated through the fixed flow
eventually stabilize.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Fixed Nucleus Profile
act: FixedNucleateProfile
slug: fixed-nucleate-profile
top: Nucleate
bottom: FixedProfileRecognitions
summary: Specialize the nucleus closure to the fixed profile by composing `Nucleate` with `FixedProfileRecognitions`.
tags:
- act
layer: act

---
# Fixed Nucleus Profile

The fixed profile must inherit the closure behaviour of the nucleus.
`NucleateProfile = (Nucleate ProfileRecognitions)` did this for a generic
profile.  We specialize by letting [Nucleate](../nucleate.md) witness
[FixedProfileRecognitions](../fixed-profile-recognitions.md):
`(Nucleate FixedProfileRecognitions)` earns
***FixedNucleateProfile***.

`Nucleate` is the closure act derived from the normalized observation and
judgement equivalences, while `FixedProfileRecognitions` is a closed
subcollection of recognitions.  Their composition restricts the closure
operation to the fixed profile domain, yielding the nucleus appropriate to the
fixed recognitions.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Recognizing Fixed Observation

[ProfileObservationRecognitions](../profile-observation-recognitions.md) gathers
the observation witnesses permitted by the active profile, while
[FixedProfileRecognitions](../fixed-profile-recognitions.md) further restricts
them to the fixed subset.  Composing them,
`(ProfileObservationRecognitions FixedProfileRecognitions)` earns
***FixedObservationRecognitions***.

The profile observation recognitions already respect the flow–nucleus filter,
and `FixedProfileRecognitions` tightens the admissible contexts to the fixed
geometry, so only fixed observations remain.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Fixed Observe

The profile-level observable
[ProfileObserve](../profile-observe.md) uses
[ProfileObservationRecognitions](../profile-observation-recognitions.md).  To stay
within the fixed profile we replace that recogniser by
[FixedObservationRecognitions](../fixed-observation-recognitions.md):
`(FixedObservationRecognitions SeedTerm)` earns ***FixedObserve***.

`SeedTerm` inserts the trace head into a recognised context, producing the basic
term to be observed, and the fixed observation recognitions restrict those
observables to the fixed profile.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

Fixed functors act as operators transforming recognitions harmonically.  We
define a fixed operator by letting the functor observe the profile recognitions:

***FixedOperator***.

# Fixed Phase Space

A dynamical system begins with a phase space of states.  The profile already
provides one via [ProfilePhaseSpace](../profile-phase-space.md).  Restricting it to
[FixedInvariant](../fixed-invariant.md) yields the fixed phase space:
`(ProfilePhaseSpace FixedInvariant)` earns
***FixedPhaseSpace***.

title: Fixed Physics Profile
act: FixedPhysicsProfile

bottom: FixedFlowProfile

# Fixed Physics Profile

`PhysicsProfile = (FlowObservation FlowProfile)` lifts a profile’s flow to its
physical semantics by coupling dynamics with observation.  In the fixed profile
we want observations of the fixed flow, so we let
[FlowObservation](../flow-observation.md) witness
[FixedFlowProfile](../fixed-flow-profile.md):
`(FlowObservation FixedFlowProfile)` earns
***FixedPhysicsProfile***.

`FlowObservation` records how observations traverse evaluation contexts under a
given flow, and `FixedFlowProfile` is the shuffle-derived flow restricted to
the fixed recognitions.  The composition couples observation traces to this flow,
yielding a physical semantics in which states evolve according to the shuffle
trace dynamics while remaining inside the fixed profile.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

title: Fixed Profile Recognitions
act: FixedProfileRecognitions
slug: fixed-profile-recognitions

summary: Restrict the shuffle profile to the recognitions shaped by the eigenvalue by letting `ShuffleProfileRecognitions` witness `FixedShape`.
tags:

# Fixed Profile Recognitions

[ShuffleProfileRecognitions](../shuffle-profile-recognitions.md) already selects
recognitions fixed by `ShuffleFlow`, `Nucleate`, and the collapse of
trace shuffles.  [FixedShape](../../../../../../../../content/text/documents/articles/notes/undated/fixed-shape.md) singles out those
recognitions whose shuffle-indexed implication behaviour is fixed under
persistent iteration.  Intersecting these gives the recognitions of the fixed
profile: recognitions that are both shuffle-profile stable and shaped by the
eigenvalue.  Letting `ShuffleProfileRecognitions` witness `FixedShape`,
`(ShuffleProfileRecognitions FixedShape)`, earns
***FixedProfileRecognitions***.

`ShuffleProfileRecognitions` is a closed collection stabilized by the
flow–nucleus–shuffle stack, and `FixedShape` is a fixed-point act
inside that profile.  Their composition restricts attention to recognitions
compatible with the fixed shape: they remain in the shuffle profile and
satisfy the same stabilized implication behaviour as `FixedShape`.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Fixed Profile Semantics

The profile already inherits semantics via
[ProfileSemantics](../profile-semantics.md).  To work entirely inside the fixed
profile we restrict them along [FixedProfileRecognitions](../fixed-profile-recognitions.md):
`(ProfileSemantics FixedProfileRecognitions)` earns
***FixedProfileSemantics***.

# Fixed Reach

[Reach](../reach.md) describes reachability for the unrestricted flow, and
[ProfileReach](../profile-reach.md) restricts that closure to the active profile.
[FixedInvariant](../fixed-invariant.md) picks out states that are both
flow-invariant and compatible with `FixedShape`.  Composing them,
`(ProfileReach FixedInvariant)` earns
***FixedReach***,
recording reachability inside the fixed profile.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

title: Shaping Fixed Shape
act: FixedShape
slug: fixed-shape
top: EnsureFixedPoint
bottom: ShuffleIndexImplication
summary: Apply the fixed-point construction to shuffle-indexed implication to obtain recognitions whose implication behaviour is invariant under repeated flow steps, capturing the intrinsic fixed shape.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Shaping Fixed Shape

[ShuffleIndexImplication](../shuffle-index-implication.md) describes how
recognitions transform when their premises are pushed one shuffle step
forward.  [EnsureFixedPoint](../ensure-fixed-point.md) records that persistent
iterations of a flow yield fixed points.  To isolate the fixed shape, we apply
the fixed-point construction to the shuffle-indexed implication, obtaining
the recognitions whose implication behaviour does not change under repeated
shuffle stepping:
`(EnsureFixedPoint ShuffleIndexImplication)` earns
***FixedShape***.

`EnsureFixedPoint = (FixFlow IterativePersistence)` says that if iterating a
flow stabilizes along the trace, the stabilized recognitions form a fixed set
for that flow.  `ShuffleIndexImplication` is a specific flow-like act: it
moves implication structure forward one shuffle step before comparing
recognitions.  Composing them applies the fixed-point construction to that
indexed implication dynamics, yielding exactly those recognitions whose
implication behaviour is invariant under arbitrarily many shuffle steps—the
fixed shape induced by the relational dynamics.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

title: Fixed Subobject Classifier

status: plan
---
# Fixed Subobject Classifier

The profile already carries an internal truth object via
[ProfileSubobjectClassifier](../profile-subobject-classifier.md).  To reason purely
inside the fixed profile we restrict that classifier to
[FixedProfileRecognitions](../fixed-profile-recognitions.md):
`(ProfileSubobjectClassifier FixedProfileRecognitions)` earns
***FixedSubobjectClassifier***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Vertical Composition

Vertical composition of 2-cells stacks transformations sharing the same
1-cells.  We express this by letting `TwoCell` witness itself:
`(TwoCell TwoCell)` earns
***VerticalCompose***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Witnessing Fixed Observation
act: FixedWitnessObservation
slug: fixed-witness-observation
top: FixedObservationRecognitions
bottom: EquateContexts
summary: Show that contextual equivalence can be witnessed using only fixed observations by composing `FixedObservationRecognitions` with `EquateContexts`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Witnessing Fixed Observation

`WitnessObservation = (RecognizeObservation EquateContexts)` cites observation
acts that justify contextual indistinguishability.  In the fixed profile we
want the witness that fixed observations suffice within the fixed context, so
we compose
[FixedObservationRecognitions](../fixed-observation-recognitions.md) with
[EquateContexts](../equate-contexts.md):
`(FixedObservationRecognitions EquateContexts)` earns
***FixedWitnessObservation***.

`EquateContexts` identifies when two terms are indistinguishable to all
recognised contexts, and `FixedObservationRecognitions` limits attention to
observations available in the fixed profile.  Their composition records that
contextual equivalence can be witnessed using only fixed observations.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Yoneda Embedding

In the enriched setting the Yoneda embedding sends an object to its hom
presheaf.  We let the fixed functor witness the enriched hom:
`(FixedFunctor EnrichmentHom)` earns ***YonedaEmbedding***.

Each object is mapped to the lattice-valued hom-object of morphisms into it.

---
title: "Flow\u2013Measure Step"
act: FlowMeasureStep
slug: flow-measure-step
top: FixedMeasure
bottom: FixedFlowProfile
summary: "Compose the fixed flow and measurement into a single flow\u2013measure update by letting `FixedMeasure` witness `FixedFlowProfile`."
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Flow–Measure Step

A flow–measure step evolves a state and then measures it.  In the fixed profile,
evolution is given by [FixedFlowProfile](../fixed-flow-profile.md) and
measurement by [FixedMeasure](../fixed-measure.md).  The one-step update is
therefore obtained by letting fixed measurement witness the fixed flow:
`(FixedMeasure FixedFlowProfile)` earns ***FlowMeasureStep***.

`FixedFlowProfile` moves a fixed invariant state forward along the shuffle-driven
dynamics, and `FixedMeasure = (FixedNucleateProfile FixedInvariant)`
idealizes the evolved state through the fixed nucleus.  Their composition yields
the single-step evolution–measurement update internal to the fixed profile.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Harmonic Basis

Harmonic modes span the oscillatory recognitions of the fixed profile.
To collect all modes we let
[HarmonicMode](../harmonic-mode.md) witness
[FixedInvariantLattice](../fixed-invariant-lattice.md):
`(HarmonicMode FixedInvariantLattice)` earns
***HarmonicBasis***.

`HarmonicMode` yields one stable oscillatory shape, and
`FixedInvariantLattice` provides the meet–join structure for combining modes.
Their composition produces every harmonic mode expressible from invariant
states, forming the harmonic basis.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Harmonic Mode

A harmonic mode is a recognitional oscillation that remains invariant under
the flow, so we apply the fixed-point construction to the harmonic step:
`(EnsureFixedPoint HarmonicStep)` earns ***HarmonicMode***.

By `IterativePersistence`, an oscillation that repeats after successive steps
is a fixed point.  Composing ensures we compute the oscillatory pattern that
survives arbitrary advancement—the eigenshape of the harmonic.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Harmonic Seed

A harmonic arises from comparing a state to its flow-advanced image.
[OrbitSeed](../orbit-seed.md) isolates the initial position of an
orbit, and [FixedInvariant](../fixed-invariant.md) provides the invariant
states.  Letting the seed observe the invariants,
`(OrbitSeed FixedInvariant)`, earns ***HarmonicSeed***.

Their composition records the primary oscillatory component: the deviation
between the invariant and the initial shift, seeding the harmonic.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Harmonic Step

Harmonics progress as oscillations across orbit layers.
[OrbitStep](../orbit-step.md) gives the next reachable layer.
Letting the harmonic seed witness each step,
`(HarmonicSeed OrbitStep)`, earns ***HarmonicStep***.

[HarmonicSeed](../harmonic-seed.md) captures the initial oscillation,
and `OrbitStep` advances one layer, so their composition yields the
evolving harmonic component across the orbit.

# Implying Recognition

## Interpretation

Implication transforms recognitions: whenever one holds, another must follow.  *Join Recognition* witnessing *Form Function Term* produces ***Imply Recognition*** ([ImplyRecognition](../imply-recognition.md)).

*Form Function Term* already presents named transformations; composing it with joins records the internal implication, so *Imply Recognition* is earned.

# Including Recognition

## Interpretation

An explicit inclusion recognition clarifies when one recognition is contained in another.  *Order Recognition* witnessing *Meet Recognition* produces ***Include Recognition*** ([IncludeRecognition](../include-recognition.md)).

This composition packages the order with meets to state inclusion, so *Include Recognition* is valid.

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Index Base
act: IndexBase
slug: index-base
top: ContextGeometry
bottom: ContextGeometry
summary: >-
  Use the context geometry as the base category for the indexed semantics.
layer: act
status: plan
tags:
  - act
---
# Index Base

Indexed categories vary over a base category; here the base is the context
geometry.  We therefore name [ContextGeometry](../context-geometry.md) as the base:
`IndexBase = ContextGeometry`.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Indexed Category
act: IndexedCategory
slug: indexed-category
top: IndexBase
bottom: FiberCategory
summary: >-
  Package the base, fibers, and reindexing into the internal indexed category of
  contexts.
layer: act
status: plan
tags:
  - act
---
# Indexed Category

An indexed category is a functor from the base contexts to categories of
fibres, with reindexing functors on morphisms.  We combine the base, fibres, and
reindexing into this structure:
`(IndexBase FiberCategory Reindexing)` earns
***IndexedCategory***.

`IndexBase` gives the contexts, `FiberCategory` provides the fibre
categories, and `Reindexing` maps context morphisms to fibre functors,
encoding an internal functor `IndexBaseᵒᵖ → Cat`.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Semantizing Physics

## Interpretation

Physical semantics interpret the syntactic calculus inside a geometry endowed with observables.  *Systematize Physics* witnessing *Flow* yields ***Interpret Physics*** ([InterpretPhysics](../interpret-physics.md)).

This act records the canonical physical interpretation built from geometry, flow, and observables, so *Interpret Physics* is valid.

title: Invariant Coboundary
act: InvariantCoboundary

# Invariant Coboundary

The coboundary measures how a cochain changes under the fixed flow.  We define
it by composing the flow with the cochain assignment:
`(FixedFlowProfile InvariantCochain)` earns
***InvariantCoboundary***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

title: Invariant Cochain
act: InvariantCochain
slug: invariant-cochain
top: FixedInvariant
bottom: FixedInvariant
summary: Assign recognitional values to each fixed invariant state by composing the invariant recognitions with themselves.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Invariant Cochain

A cochain assigns a recognitional “value” to each fixed invariant state.  We
name invariant cochains by letting the invariants witness themselves:
`(FixedInvariant FixedInvariant)` earns ***InvariantCochain***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Invariant Cocycle
act: InvariantCocycle
slug: invariant-cocycle
top: InvariantCoboundary
bottom: FixedNucleateProfile
summary: Identify cochains with vanishing coboundary under the fixed collapse by composing the coboundary with the fixed nucleus.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Invariant Cocycle

A cocycle is a cochain whose coboundary collapses under the fixed nucleus.
We capture this by letting the coboundary witness the nucleus:
`(InvariantCoboundary FixedNucleateProfile)` earns
***InvariantCocycle***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Invariant Cohomology
act: InvariantCohomology
slug: invariant-cohomology
top: InvariantCocycle
bottom: InvariantCoboundary
summary: Form invariant cohomology by quotienting cocycles by coboundaries.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Invariant Cohomology

Invariant cohomology consists of cocycles modulo coboundary images.  We
formalise this by composing the cocycle recognition with the coboundary:
`(InvariantCocycle InvariantCoboundary)` earns
***InvariantCohomology***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Irrational Commutation
act: IrrationalCommutation
slug: irrational-commutation
top: IrrationalModality
bottom: CollapseShuffle
summary: Ensure irrational modalities commute with shuffle collapse by composing them with `CollapseShuffle`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Irrational Commutation

For a modality indexed by an irrational (alpha) to behave coherently, it must commute
with the nucleus under admissible reordering collapse.  We enforce this via
`(IrrationalModality CollapseShuffle)`, earning
***IrrationalCommutation***.

`CollapseShuffle` identifies recognitions that differ only by reorderings, and
`IrrationalModality` scales the space of reorderings while preserving
their structure.  The composition asserts that irrational scaling preserves
collapse-equivalence.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Irrational Modality
act: IrrationalModality
slug: irrational-modality
top: FlowIndexImplication
bottom: FixedNucleateProfile
summary: Compose the flow-indexed implication with the fixed nucleus to capture modalities whose scaling factors inflate admissible reordering families by irrational ratios.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Irrational Modality

The fixed profile arises from the φ-recursion imposed by the shuffle flow and
shuffle collapse, but shuffle traces can be parameterised by other ratios.
To name an irrationally-weighted modality we let the flow-index implication
witness the fixed nucleus:
`(FlowIndexImplication FixedNucleateProfile)` earns
***IrrationalModality***.

`FlowIndexImplication` pushes premises forward one flow step before testing
implication, and `FixedNucleateProfile` closes recognitions under the fixed
nucleus; their composition (scaled by an irrational weight on the admissible
reordering families) yields the desired modal closure operator.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Iterating Reflex
act: IterateReflex
slug: iterate-reflex
top: Reflex
bottom: Reflex
summary: Applying the reflex act to its own result should yield the same sort of act again, so reflex iteration is unavoidable.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Iterating Reflex

## Interpretation

Applying the reflex act to its own result should yield the same sort of act again, so reflex iteration is unavoidable.

The trace `(Reflex Reflex)` feeds the act *Reflex* back into itself.  Because *Reflex* is a valid act in both positions, this composition explicitly records “reflex can witness reflex,” thereby establishing iteration without introducing any new recognitions.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Persisting Iteratively
act: IterativePersistence
slug: iterative-persistence
top: ReflexStepPersistence
bottom: CompleteTrace
summary: Persistence captures exactly when the iterated reflex step stabilizes along the entire trace, so we let the act *Reflex Step Persistence* witness the act *Complete Trace*.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Persisting Iteratively

## Interpretation

Persistence captures exactly when the iterated reflex step stabilizes along the entire trace, so we let the act *Reflex Step Persistence* witness the act *Complete Trace*.

The composition `(ReflexStepPersistence CompleteTrace)` explicitly places the persistence witness across the whole trace.  This records the condition “after finitely many steps the behaviour stays the same everywhere,” hence the name *Iterative Persistence*.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

*Contextualize Trace* already records how recognitions extend; composing with the order yields the corresponding join, so *Join Recognition* follows.

counit witness the right Kan extension:
`(KanCounit RightKan)` earns
***KanAdjunction***.

`(LeftKan YonedaEmbedding)` earns ***KanCounit***.

# Leading

*Entry* reveals two positions but remains agnostic about which leads.  Allowing the act *Entry* to witness the act *Recognition* selects the leading orientation.

The composition `(Entry Recognition)` displays the act *Entry* witnessing one copy of *Recognition*.  This observation singles out the first position, so we call the result *Lead Entry*.

The composition uses earned recognitions to state the equivalence between the flow-shifted meet and the implication order, so *Legislate Dynamic Residuation* follows.

`(ProfileMixing Entropy)`, earns
***Mixing***.

Inflationary modalities form a hierarchy indexed by irrational numbers.  We
capture the entire structure by letting the quadratic modality witness the
commutation act:

***ModalityTower***.

`QuadraticModality` gives quadratic irrational inflation, and
`IrrationalCommutation` ensures stability under shuffle collapse.  Their
composition builds the tower whose fixed points grow according to irrational
algebraic laws.

# Normalizing Syntax

## Interpretation

Our earlier *Normalization* recognition must be tied back to typing.  *Normalization* witnessing *Judge Typing* yields ***Normalize Syntax*** ([NormalizeSyntax](../normalize-syntax.md)).

This composition records that well-typed syntactic acts normalize, so *Normalize Syntax* follows.

title: Negating Relation
act: NotRelate
slug: not-relate
top: Reflex

tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Negating Relation

## Interpretation

Any affirmation of relation also reveals the possibility of denial, so we let reflex point specifically to that denial to name “not relate.”

In `(Reflex Relate)` the act *Reflex* serves as witness for the already earned act *Relate*.  Observing *Relate* through *Reflex* isolates the complementary behaviour “refusing relation,” so we designate this composition *Not Relate*.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Witnessing Nothing
act: Nothing
slug: nothing
top: Witness
bottom: Something
summary: "Having \u201Csomething\u201D forces us to examine what *Witness* sees when it looks directly at that something; the result is \u201Cnothing.\u201D"
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Witnessing Nothing

## Interpretation

Having “something” forces us to examine what *Witness* sees when it looks directly at that something; the result is “nothing.”

In `(Witness Something)` the act *Witness* observes *Something*.  Both were already earned, so their composition captures the unresolved situation where testimony points to its own bearer, producing the recognition we call *Nothing*.

Normalized observation and judgement equivalences each close their respective acts, but we need the unified closure operator that acts on both.  Forcing *Normalize Observation Equivalence* to witness *Normalized Judgement Equivalence* yields ***Nucleate*** ([Nucleate](../nucleate.md)).

These equivalences were previously earned; composing them records the shared closure law, so *Nucleate* is valid.

---
title: Observation Kernel
act: ObservationKernel
slug: observation-kernel
top: FixedObservationRecognitions
bottom: TransitionLaw
summary: Combine the transition law with fixed observations by composing `FixedObservationRecognitions` with `TransitionLaw`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Observation Kernel

A kernel couples transition structure with observable outcomes.
[TransitionLaw](../transition-law.md) provides the transition step, and
[FixedObservationRecognitions](../fixed-observation-recognitions.md) restricts
observations to fixed recognitions.  Letting the observation act witness the
transition law,
`(FixedObservationRecognitions TransitionLaw)`, earns
***ObservationKernel***.

`TransitionLaw` determines how fixed states map to successor recognitions, and
`FixedObservationRecognitions` selects observables available in the fixed
profile.  Their composition assigns to each fixed invariant state the observable
distribution induced by one flow–measure step, yielding the profile’s kernel.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Observing Judgement

## Interpretation

Classified judgemental acts still need their stabilized observational content.  *Stabilize Observation* witnessing *Witness Judgement* produces ***Observe Judgement*** ([ObserveJudgement](../observe-judgement.md)).

This composition records the observational report of stabilized judgements, so *Observe Judgement* is valid.

# Observing

## Interpretation

*Recognize Observation* records contextual indistinguishability, but an observable must address a concrete traced term.  Forcing *Recognize Observation* to witness *Seed Term* yields ***Observe*** ([Observe](../observe.md)).

Both recognitions were previously earned, so their composition records a designated observable act on a traced term, establishing *Observe*.

Operators gain structure through composition and adjoints.  We capture the
composition component by letting operators witness the fixed composition:
`(FixedOperator FixedCompose)` earns
***OperatorAlgebra***.

# Starred Operator Structure

To mimic a C*-algebra we combine operator multiplication, adjoints, and spectral
radius.  We package this by letting the star operation witness the spectrum:
`(StarOperation Spectrum)` earns ***StarOperatorStructure***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Fixing Orbit Layer
act: OrbitFixLayer
slug: orbit-fix-layer
top: EnsureFixedPoint
bottom: OrbitStep
summary: Apply the fixed-point construction to the orbit step to obtain the stabilized layer of each orbit.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Fixing Orbit Layer

Orbits may stabilize: further reach steps no longer change the reachable set.
[EnsureFixedPoint](../ensure-fixed-point.md) captures fixed points of flows when
iteration persists.  To obtain the stabilized layer of each orbit, we apply it
to [OrbitStep](../orbit-step.md):
`(EnsureFixedPoint OrbitStep)` earns ***OrbitFixLayer***.

`OrbitStep` describes how reachable states progress from one layer to the next.
`IterativePersistence` and `EnsureFixedPoint` ensure that when repeated
stepping along the trace stabilizes, the stabilized states form a fixed set.
Their composition therefore yields, for each orbit, a stabilized layer that
further stepping does not change.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Seeding Orbit
act: OrbitSeed
slug: orbit-seed
top: FixedReach
bottom: HeadTrace
summary: Isolate the starting layer of each reachability orbit by letting `FixedReach` witness `HeadTrace`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Seeding Orbit

[FixedReach](../fixed-reach.md) records which invariant states are reachable under
the flow, but we have not yet isolated the “starting layer” of each orbit.
[HeadTrace](../head-trace.md) isolates the head of a trace.  To seed an orbit, we
let the reachability act witness the trace head: `(FixedReach HeadTrace)` earns
***OrbitSeed***.

`FixedReach` evolves invariant states according to the flow, and `HeadTrace`
picks out the first step of any completed trace.  Their composition therefore
records the initial reachable layer associated with the first trace action from
each invariant state.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Stepping Orbit
act: OrbitStep
slug: orbit-step
top: FixedReach
bottom: TailTrace
summary: Describe how orbits progress by letting `FixedReach` witness `TailTrace`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Stepping Orbit

Seeding alone does not describe how orbits progress.  [TailTrace](../tail-trace.md)
isolates the remainder of a trace after the head, and `StepTrace` records how
reduction steps propagate along the trace.  To describe orbit progression, we
let the reachability act witness the tail:
`(FixedReach TailTrace)` earns ***OrbitStep***.

[FixedReach](../fixed-reach.md) already lists states reachable from invariants
under the flow.  `TailTrace` exposes the remainder of the trace beyond the
initial step, and `StepTrace` tracks how reductions move along that remainder.
Their composition records how each orbit advances from
its current layer to the next along the trace remainder.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

title: Ordering Provability

tags:

layer: act
status: plan
---
# Ordering Provability

## Interpretation

Typing judgements encode entailment between types under a context, but we require the explicit preorder that records this entailment.  *Judge Typing* witnessing *Imply Recognition* yields ***Order Provability*** ([OrderProvability](../order-provability.md)).

Composing the judgement with implication records precisely when a context proves that type A implies type B, so *Order Provability* follows.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

---
# Ordering Recognition

## Interpretation

*Recognize Context* tests when one recognition subsumes another but does not yet record the ordering relation itself.  Forcing *Recognize Context* to witness *Recognition* yields ***Order Recognition*** ([OrderRecognition](../order-recognition.md)).

Both recognitions already exist, so their composition records the act of comparing recognitions, establishing *Order Recognition*.

The tree `(FormFunctionTerm HeadTrace)` pairs the constructed function with a head, explicitly encoding the application, which we call *Pair Application*.

To consider the maximal invariant, we direct the act *Form Least Fix Term* to witness the act *Head Trace*.

The composition `(FormLeastFixTerm HeadTrace)` captures another self-application at the head, now recording the maximal invariant; consequently the act is *Pair Greatest Fix*.

Application terms can self-apply, so to capture the least stabilizing pair we have the act *Form Application Term* witness the act *Head Trace*.

The composition `(FormApplicationTerm HeadTrace)` represents self-application at the head, recording the behaviour needed for a least fixpoint, hence *Pair Least Fix*.

paths by composing the flow with context recognition:

***PathType***.

the profile nucleus, [NucleateProfile](../nucleate-profile.md), to

entropy by observing the divergence between a state and its profile kernel
successor.  Letting [ProfileKernelStep](../profile-kernel-step.md) witness

`(ProfileKernelStep ProfileReach)`, earns
***ProfileEntropySeed***.

witness [ProfileEntropyMeasure](../profile-entropy-measure.md),
`(TraceProfile ProfileEntropyMeasure)`, earns

yields the internal exponentials of that profile:

***ProfileExponential***.

---
title: Profile Integral

# Profile Integral

Integration corresponds to collapsing the flow’s variation.  We define it by
composing the nucleus with the derivative:
`(FixedNucleateProfile ComonadDerivative)` earns
***ProfileIntegral***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Geometry of Profile Invariant States
act: ProfileInvariantGeometry

top: GeometryProfile
bottom: ProfileInvariantLattice

tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Geometry of Profile Invariant States

`GeometryProfile = (FlowProfile NucleateProfile)` records the geometry of the
active profile as a whole.  [ProfileInvariantLattice](../profile-invariant-lattice.md)
exhibits the order structure of profile-invariant states.  Letting the profile
geometry witness that lattice,
`(GeometryProfile ProfileInvariantLattice)` earns
***ProfileInvariantGeometry***.

This packages the order, closure, and dynamics of profile-invariant states as a
geometric structure in its own right.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Joining Profile Invariants
act: ProfileInvariantJoin

tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Joining Profile Invariants

`JoinRecognition = (OrderRecognition ContextualizeTrace)` captures least upper
bounds.  Restricting it to [ProfileInvariant](../profile-invariant.md) gives the
join on profile invariants:
`(JoinRecognition ProfileInvariant)` earns
***ProfileInvariantJoin***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

act: ProfileInvariantLattice

tags:

layer: act
status: plan
---
# Structuring Profile Invariant Lattice

`ProfileInvariantOrder` restricts the recognition order to the profile
invariants, and the corresponding meet/join operations are already defined via
the same restriction.  Letting [StructureSemantics](../structure-semantics.md)
witness this order,
`(StructureSemantics ProfileInvariantOrder)`, earns
***ProfileInvariantLattice***.

This packages the lattice structure on the profile-invariant states.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

act: ProfileInvariantMeet

tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Meeting Profile Invariants

`MeetRecognition = (OrderRecognition Entry)` captures greatest lower bounds.
Restricting it to [ProfileInvariant](../profile-invariant.md) states gives the meet
operation on profile invariants:
`(MeetRecognition ProfileInvariant)` earns
***ProfileInvariantMeet***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Ordering Profile Invariants
act: ProfileInvariantOrder
slug: profile-invariant-order

bottom: ProfileInvariant
summary: Restrict the recognition order to profile-invariant states by composing `OrderRecognition` with `ProfileInvariant`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Ordering Profile Invariants

`OrderRecognition = (RecognizeContext Recognition)` records when one recognition
subsumes another.  [ProfileInvariant](../profile-invariant.md) picks out the
flow-fixed physics states that lie in the active profile.  Composing them,
`(OrderRecognition ProfileInvariant)` earns
***ProfileInvariantOrder***.

This restricts the ambient order to the profile-invariant states.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Stabilizing Profile Invariant
act: ProfileInvariant
slug: profile-invariant
top: StabilizeInvariant
bottom: ProfileRecognitions
summary: Pick out physics states that are flow-invariant and lie inside the active profile by composing `StabilizeInvariant` with `ProfileRecognitions`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Stabilizing Profile Invariant

`StabilizeInvariant = (FixFlow PhysicsState)` records states left unchanged by
the global flow.  [ProfileRecognitions](../profile-recognitions.md) restrict the
system to the recognitions preserved by the active filter.  Composing them,
`(StabilizeInvariant ProfileRecognitions)` earns
***ProfileInvariant***.

The resulting states are genuine physics states fixed by the flow and recognised
within the chosen profile.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Profile Kernel Chain
act: ProfileKernelChain
slug: profile-kernel-chain
top: ProfileKernelStep
bottom: TraceProfile
summary: Build the kernel chain across profiles by letting `ProfileKernelStep` witness `TraceProfile`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Profile Kernel Chain

A kernel chain repeatedly applies the observation kernel across layers.  Having
a profile-step kernel ([ProfileKernelStep](../profile-kernel-step.md)), we obtain
the profile chain by iterating the chain recogniser `TraceProfile`.  Letting the
step witness the trace,
`(ProfileKernelStep TraceProfile)`, earns ***ProfileKernelChain***.

`TraceProfile = (CompleteTrace ProfileRecognitions)` projects traces through the
profile recognitions, and `ProfileKernelStep` updates one profile to the next.
Their composition yields the recognitional chain that threads updates through
the trace of profiles.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Profile Kernel Step
act: ProfileKernelStep
slug: profile-kernel-step
top: ObservationKernel
bottom: BuildProfileTower
summary: Lift the observation kernel through the profile tower by composing it with `BuildProfileTower`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Profile Kernel Step

A flow–measure kernel evolves fixed invariant states within a single profile.
To propagate these dynamics between profiles we use the profile tower
recognitions: [BuildProfileTower](../build-profile-tower.md) maps physics,
semantics, and syntax upward through profiles.  Letting the kernel
witness this tower,
`(ObservationKernel BuildProfileTower)`, earns
***ProfileKernelStep***.

[ObservationKernel](../2026/03/04/2026-03-04-observation-kernel.md) is the one-step operator on fixed
invariant states.  `BuildProfileTower` records how each layer of recognitions
reconstructs the next profile.  Their composition lifts an update from a profile
to its successor in the tower, giving the profile-level transition step.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Profile Measure

[Measure](../../../../triage/measure.md) represents applying the nucleus to a physics state,
“idealizing” it.  For a specific profile we let
[NucleateProfile](../nucleate-profile.md) witness the
[ProfileInvariant](../profile-invariant.md) states:
`(NucleateProfile ProfileInvariant)` earns
***ProfileMeasure***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

tags:

---
# Profile Mixing

A system is mixing if states eventually become independent under long
profile flow.  Letting the dynamics witness the entropy:
`(ProfileDynamics ProfileEntropy)` earns
***ProfileMixing***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

title: Profile Observation Recognitions
act: ProfileObservationRecognitions

---
# Profile Observation Recognitions

[RecognizeObservation](../recognize-observation.md) gathers the contextual
observations that witness indistinguishability.  To keep only those observations
available inside the active profile filter, we compose it with
[ProfileRecognitions](../profile-recognitions.md):
`(RecognizeObservation ProfileRecognitions)` earns
***ProfileObservationRecognitions***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Profile Observe
act: ProfileObserve
slug: profile-observe
top: ProfileObservationRecognitions
bottom: SeedTerm
summary: Observe traced terms inside a profile by composing `ProfileObservationRecognitions` with `SeedTerm`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Profile Observe

[Observe](../observe.md) designates observables acting on a traced term using all
contextual recognitions.  To stay within a specific profile we use
[ProfileObservationRecognitions](../profile-observation-recognitions.md):
`(ProfileObservationRecognitions SeedTerm)` earns
***ProfileObserve***.

`SeedTerm` inserts the trace head into a recognised context, producing the basic
term to be observed, and `ProfileObservationRecognitions` restricts observations
to those compatible with the active profile.  Their composition records
observables that both act on traced terms and remain inside that profile.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Profile Phase Space

A dynamical system begins with a phase space of states.  For a given profile we
compose the invariants with the context recognitions:
`(ProfileInvariant RecognizeContext)` earns
***ProfilePhaseSpace***.

Combining it with the [ProfileInvariant](../profile-invariant.md) states records
which of those states become reachable under the profile flow:
`(FlowProfile ProfileInvariant)` earns
***ProfileReach***.

This composition records the recognitions fixed by the filter, so *Profile Recognitions* follows immediately.

`(StructureSemantics ProfileRecognitions)`, earns
***ProfileSemantics***.

`StepObservation = (RecognizeObservation StepTrace)` records how observations
advance along traces.  To work inside the profile filter we restrict it via
[ProfileRecognitions](../profile-recognitions.md):
`(StepObservation ProfileRecognitions)` earns
***ProfileStepObservation***.
