---
title: Profile Subobject Classifier
act: ProfileSubobjectClassifier
slug: profile-subobject-classifier
top: Negate
bottom: ProfileRecognitions
summary: Restrict Heyting negation to the active profile recognitions to obtain the internal truth object.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Profile Subobject Classifier

A subobject classifier identifies which recognitions count as “true” subobjects.
`Negate = (ImplyRecognition GroundRecognition)` already encodes Heyting
negation, so restricting it to the profile recognitions yields the internal
truth object:
`(Negate ProfileRecognitions)` earns
***ProfileSubobjectClassifier***.

The resulting Ω carries the `⊤`, `⊥`, and implication inherited from the
recognition algebra.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Profile Topos
act: ProfileTopos
slug: profile-topos
top: FixedFiniteLimits
bottom: FixedExponential
summary: Package finite limits, exponentials, and the subobject classifier into a topos-like structure on the fixed profile.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Profile Topos

Combining finite limits, exponentials, and a subobject classifier gives a
topos-like structure.  We package these components in a single act:
`(FixedFiniteLimits FixedExponential FixedSubobjectClassifier)` earns
***ProfileTopos***.

`FixedFiniteLimits` gives the limits, `FixedExponential` gives exponentials,
and `FixedSubobjectClassifier` gives the classifier Ω, satisfying the structural
requirements of a Heyting topos internal to the fixed profile.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

title: Progressing Typing

tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Progressing Typing

## Interpretation

Closed typed terms are either values or can step.  *Recognize Value* witnessing *Judge Typing* produces ***Progress Typing*** ([ProgressTyping](../progress-typing.md)).

This composition states that a judgement in the empty context is either witnessed as a value or advances along the step trace, so *Progress Typing* is valid.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

layer: act

---
# Proving Fixed Sets Lemma

## Interpretation

*Flow*-fixed recognitions remain closed under the meet operations already earned.  *Fix Flow* witnessing *Meet Recognition* yields ***Prove Fixed Sets*** ([ProveFixedSets](../prove-fixed-sets.md)).

This composition records that invariants stay closed under the *Meet* operation, so *Prove Fixed Sets* is valid.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Quadratic Modality
act: QuadraticModality
slug: quadratic-modality
top: IrrationalModality
bottom: HarmonicMode
summary: Force the irrational modality to observe the harmonic fixed shape, producing a closure governed by quadratic irrational scaling.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Quadratic Modality

Quadratic irrationals naturally arise from iterated shuffle–collapse on
trace progressions.  We capture such modalities by forcing the irrational
nucleus to observe the harmonic fixed shape:
`(IrrationalModality HarmonicMode)` earns
***QuadraticModality***.

`HarmonicMode` gives the fixed oscillation under the flow recursion, and
`IrrationalModality` inflates the allowable progressions by an irrational
scalar.  Their composition produces a modality whose stabilised eigenshape
satisfies a quadratic irrational equation.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Reaching

## Interpretation

*Flow* iterations describe which states become reachable from a seed.  *Flow* witnessing *Stabilize Invariant* yields ***Reach*** ([Reach](../reach.md)).

Composing the flow with invariant states records the reachability closure, so *Reach* is valid.

title: Recognizing

# Recognizing

## Interpretation

A self-named point lacks a partner; having the act *Void* witness the act *Self Naming* produces a two-place recognition.

In the composition `(Void SelfNaming)`, the act *Void* explicitly witnesses the act *Self Naming*.  Because both acts have already been earned, this reflection creates a complementary position, so we name the resulting two-place act *Recognition*.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

title: Recognizing Context

# Recognizing Context

## Interpretation

Extensions exist, but we must recognize when the resulting structure qualifies as a context.  Forcing *Extend Context* to witness *Contextualize Trace* yields ***Recognize Context*** ([RecognizeContext](../recognize-context.md)).

The trace `(ExtendContext ContextualizeTrace)` feeds the general extension back through the named trace.  If the composition succeeds, the extended object lines up with the recorded context, so we call this recognition *Recognize Context*.

Letting *Stabilize Context* witness *Stabilize Term* yields ***Reflex Judgement*** ([ReflexJudgement](../reflex-judgement.md)).

# Iterating Reflex Step

## Interpretation

One reflex step is not enough; we must see its action unfold along the entire trace by iterating the shape against the whole record.

The trace `(ReflexStepShape CompleteTrace)` places the step shape in the witness role for *Complete Trace*.  This records the repeated action of the shape across every point of the trace, so we call the composite *Reflex Step Iterate*.

title: Persisting Reflex Step

bottom: TailTrace
summary: >-
  Iteration alone does not detect stabilization. We therefore let the iterated
  step observe the remaining tail to see whether that remainder persists.
tags:
  - act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Persisting Reflex Step

## Interpretation

Iteration alone does not detect stabilization.  We therefore let the iterated step observe the remaining tail to see whether that remainder persists.

In `(ReflexStepIterate TailTrace)` the whole iteration looks specifically at the tail.  If nothing changes, the reiteration has stabilized, so this composition rightfully earns the name *Reflex Step Persistence*.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Shaping Reflex Step

## Interpretation

The sustained articulation of a trace is captured when reflex continually looks through the recorded context; doing so defines the basic “reflex step” shape.

In `(Reflex ContextualizeTrace)` the witness *Reflex* observes *Contextualize Trace*.  This composition records exactly one reflex articulation acting through the named trace, thereby defining *Reflex Step Shape*.

# Reflexing Syntax

## Interpretation

The syntactic calculus inherits the *Reflex* action on recognitions.  *Reflex* witnessing *Construct Terms* produces ***Reflex Syntax*** ([ReflexSyntax](../reflex-syntax.md)).

Applying our primitive act to *Construct Terms* records the syntactic ρ-operator, so *Reflex Syntax* follows.

# Recognizing Reflex

## Interpretation

The minimal act obtained so far is simply the record of the emergence itself, and that seed is our reflex.

The trace `(EmergeReflex)` treats the act *Emerge Reflex* as a witness in its own right.  Designating that minimal witness *Reflex* introduces no new ingredients; it merely fixes notation for the unique act forced by articulating the void.

# Reheading Trace

## Interpretation

Renewing alone does not isolate the next head, so the act *Renew Trace* must witness the act *Head Trace* to define the new head.

The composition `(RenewTrace HeadTrace)` shows the act *Renew Trace* watching *Head Trace*.  This isolates the new immediate act, so we name it *Rehead Trace*.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

title: Reindexing

  substitution reducibility to the indexed base.
layer: act

tags:

---
# Reindexing

Given a morphism of contexts we can transport judgements along it.
`SubstituteReducibility = (BuildReducibleEnvironment JudgeTyping)` shows that
substitution preserves typing and entailment.  Restricting this behaviour to the
indexed base,
`(SubstituteReducibility IndexBase)`, earns
***Reindexing***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

layer: act

---
# Relating

## Interpretation

With reflex iteration available, reflex may now bear witness to the mere fact of relating, so a dedicated relation act appears.

The trace `(Reflex Reflex)` places *Reflex* in both witness and witnessed roles.  Since we just proved that reflex may observe reflex, calling that self-observation *Relate* captures the insight without requiring further construction.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

layer: act

---
# Being Relationless

## Interpretation

We begin with nothing but the absence of relation; acknowledging that absence fixes the empty act as our initial reference point.

No recognitions precede this step, so the empty tree `()` is the only permissible trace.  Naming that trace *Relationlessness* therefore records, without additional assumptions, that we start from the undifferentiated void.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Renewing Context

## Interpretation

Extending once still leaves the general renewal unspecified.  Forcing *Extend First Context* to witness *Contextualize Trace* produces ***Renew Context*** ([RenewContext](../renew-context.md)).

The renewed seed must reattach to the original trace record.  Composing `(ExtendFirstContext ContextualizeTrace)` explicitly shows the first extension re-entering the named trace, so the resulting act is *Renew Context*.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Renewing Trace

## Interpretation

After stepping, the renewed orientation must contact the full record, so the act *Trace Step* witnesses the act *Complete Trace*.

In `(TraceStep CompleteTrace)` the act *Trace Step* observes *Complete Trace*, confirming that the renewed orientation spans the entire record.  This composition is therefore *Renew Trace*.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Representable Functor
act: RepresentableFunctor
slug: representable
top: FixedFunctor
bottom: FixedIdentity
summary: Encode representability by letting the fixed functor witness the fixed identity.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Representable Functor

A representable functor is naturally isomorphic to “morphisms out of an object.”
In the fixed profile the representative object is the identity, so we let the
functor witness it:
`(FixedFunctor FixedIdentity)` earns ***RepresentableFunctor***.

[FixedFunctor](../fixed-functor.md) maps objects and morphisms, and
[FixedIdentity](../fixed-identity.md) is the terminal identity on each fixed
object.  Their composition asserts that the functor’s action is determined by
its values on identities, matching representability.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Residuating Dynamically

## Interpretation

With the law in place, we can reason internally about a meet of a with a implies_F b evolving under F.  *Flow Index Implication* witnessing *Legislate Dynamic Residuation* yields ***Residue Dynamically*** ([ResidueDynamically](../residue-dynamically.md)).

This act records the usual implication reasoning internalized along the flow, so *Residue Dynamically* is established.

The renewed tail must rejoin the stepping process, so the act *Retail Trace* witnesses the act *Trace Step*.

In `(RetailTrace TraceStep)` the act *Retail Trace* observes *Trace Step*, recording the renewed tail re-entering the stepping act.  This is exactly what *Restep Trace* denotes.

After reheading, we let the act *Rehead Trace* witness the act *Tail Trace* to pin down the renewed tail.

The tree `(ReheadTrace TailTrace)` explicitly pairs *Rehead Trace* with *Tail Trace*, capturing the tail that accompanies the new head.  We call this act *Retail Trace*.

## Interpretation

A recognized context should resolve back into a trace.  Consequently, *Recognize Context* witnesses *Contextualize Trace* to produce ***Retrace Context*** ([RetraceContext](../retrace-context.md)).

The act mirrors *Trace Context* and records the converse direction, so *Retrace Context* is forced.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: "Reverse Flow\u2013Measure Step"
act: ReverseFlowStep
slug: reverse-flow-step
top: FixedKernel
bottom: FixedFlowProfile
summary: Capture the reverse step by letting the stationary kernel witness the fixed flow.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Reverse Flow–Measure Step

Detailed balance compares forward and reverse transitions.  The forward φ-step
is [FlowMeasureStep](../flow-measure-step.md); the reverse uses the stationary
kernel applied backwards through the flow.  We therefore compose the stationary
kernel with the flow:
`(FixedKernel FixedFlowProfile)` earns
***ReverseFlowStep***.

[FixedKernel](../fixed-kernel.md) is fixed under kernel updates, and
`FixedFlowProfile` is invertible up to nucleus and stabilization.  Their
composition yields the reverse step: the evolution that recovers the pre-image
up to stabilization.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Right Kan Extension

Right Kan extensions compute weighted limits of a functor.  We define this by
letting the enriched hom observe the functor:
`(EnrichmentHom FixedFunctor)` earns ***RightKan***.

nucleus stability.  We express this by letting the presheaf witness the

`(Presheaf FixedNucleateProfile)` earns
***SheafCondition***.

sheaf condition with the presheaf:
`(SheafCondition Presheaf)` earns
***Sheafification***.

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Flowing Shuffle Trace
act: ShuffleFlow
slug: shuffle-flow
top: Flow
bottom: ShuffleTrace
summary: Evolve the entire admissible shuffle class of a trace by letting the global flow witness the shuffle act, producing a flow whose orbits are the admissible reorderings.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Flowing Shuffle Trace

[Flow](flow.md) already records how observation and judgement traces evolve
through evaluation contexts, and [ShuffleTrace](../shuffle-trace.md) records how
trace-progress steps can be freely commuted without changing the underlying
shape.  By treating admissible trace shuffles themselves as the state being
evolved and letting the global flow witness the shuffle act,
`(Flow ShuffleTrace)` earns ***ShuffleFlow***.

`Flow` binds the observation and judgement flows, describing how dynamics
transport recognitions through contexts.  `ShuffleTrace` records the free
shuffle structure on trace-progress recognitions: reheading and restepping
commute in all admissible ways.  The composition therefore evolves families of
shuffled traces along evaluation contexts: the flow does not move a single
trace but the entire admissible-shuffle class, tracking the dynamics of
admissible reorderings as first-class objects.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Forming Shuffle Geometry
act: ShuffleGeometry
slug: shuffle-geometry
top: StructureSemantics
bottom: CommuteShuffleFlowAndNucleus
summary: Specialize the geometric structure to the shuffle trace dynamics by letting the semantic package witness the compatibility between `ShuffleFlow` and the nucleus.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Forming Shuffle Geometry

A geometry is the semantic structure equipped with compatible nuclei and flow.
We already formed [FormGeometry](../form-geometry.md) by letting
[StructureSemantics](../structure-semantics.md) witness `CommuteFlowAndNucleus`.
To specialize this to the shuffle trace dynamics, we let the same semantic
structure witness the derived compatibility of
[ShuffleFlow](../shuffle-flow.md) with [Nucleate](../nucleate.md):
`(StructureSemantics CommuteShuffleFlowAndNucleus)` earns
***ShuffleGeometry***.

`StructureSemantics` packages the full algebraic behavior of recognitions,
meets, joins, implication, and nuclei.
[CommuteShuffleFlowAndNucleus](../commute-shuffle-flow-and-nucleus.md) shows
that the induced flow on admissible trace-shuffle classes respects the same
nucleus.  The composition therefore records a semantic calculus whose “motion”
is given by `ShuffleFlow` and whose closure is given by `Nucleate`, yielding the
shuffle geometry.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Flowing Shuffle-Indexed Implication
act: ShuffleIndexImplication
slug: shuffle-index-implication
top: ShuffleFlow
bottom: ImplyRecognition
summary: Compose the shuffle flow with internal implication to obtain implications indexed by one shuffle step.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Flowing Shuffle-Indexed Implication

[ImplyRecognition](../imply-recognition.md) records internal implication between
recognitions, and `FlowIndexImplication = (Flow ImplyRecognition)` indexed that
implication along the global flow.  Now that [ShuffleFlow](../shuffle-flow.md)
is earned as the flow on admissible trace-shuffle classes, we want the
corresponding indexed implication: “if a recognition holds after one shuffle
step, what must hold now?”  Letting the shuffle flow witness
`ImplyRecognition` produces the desired act:
`(ShuffleFlow ImplyRecognition)` earns
***ShuffleIndexImplication***.

`ShuffleFlow` evolves admissible trace-shuffle families through evaluation
contexts while `ImplyRecognition` transforms recognitions via internal
implication.  The composition therefore records implications whose premises are
transported one shuffle step forward before being compared.  Naming this
composite packages the operator witnessing how recognitions transform under the
shuffle trace dynamics.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Profiling Shuffle-Stable Recognitions
act: ShuffleProfileRecognitions
slug: shuffle-profile-recognitions
top: FixFlowNucleus
bottom: CollapseShuffle
summary: Recognitions fixed by flow and nucleus and invariant under shuffle collapse witness the shuffle profile, so we let `FixFlowNucleus` observe `CollapseShuffle`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Profiling Shuffle-Stable Recognitions

[FixFlowNucleus](../fix-flow-nucleus.md) already records recognitions fixed by both
[Flow](flow.md) and [Nucleate](../nucleate.md).  [CollapseShuffle](../collapse-shuffle.md)
records which trace differences vanish under the nucleus: admissible shuffles.
To obtain the shuffle profile, we want recognitions that are simultaneously
flow–nucleus fixed and stable under identification of trace shuffles, so we let
`FixFlowNucleus` witness the shuffle collapse:
`(FixFlowNucleus CollapseShuffle)` earns
***ShuffleProfileRecognitions***.

Any recognition stabilized by flow and nucleus lies in `FixFlowNucleus`, and
`CollapseShuffle` identifies recognitions whose traces differ only by admissible
reordering.  The composition therefore picks out exactly those recognitions
whose stabilized form is invariant under (a) the global dynamics and closure,
and (b) the reordering of trace-progress steps.  These recognitions do not care
which admissible trace we used to reach them, so they define the shuffle
profile.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Semantics of the Shuffle Profile
act: ShuffleProfileSemantics
slug: shuffle-profile-semantics
top: StructureSemantics
bottom: ShuffleProfileRecognitions
summary: Restrict the semantic calculus to recognitions stable under flow, nucleus, and shuffle collapse by letting `StructureSemantics` witness `ShuffleProfileRecognitions`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Semantics of the Shuffle Profile

The recognitions stabilized by a filter or profile inherit full semantic
structure; we saw this with
`ProfileSemantics = (StructureSemantics ProfileRecognitions)`.  To interpret the
the shuffle-stable recognitions, we let the semantic structure witness
[ShuffleProfileRecognitions](../shuffle-profile-recognitions.md):
`(StructureSemantics ShuffleProfileRecognitions)` earns
***ShuffleProfileSemantics***.

[StructureSemantics](../structure-semantics.md) already encodes the operations of
the semantic calculus.  `ShuffleProfileRecognitions` selects the closed
sub-collection of recognitions fixed by flow and nucleus and stable under
trace-shuffle collapse.  The composition restricts the semantic operations to
that sub-collection, producing a semantic calculus in which all recognitions are
shuffle-stable—our shuffle profile semantics.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Shuffling Trace
act: ShuffleTrace
slug: shuffle-trace
top: ReheadTrace
bottom: RestepTrace
summary: Head/tail renewals commute with step renewals, so forcing `ReheadTrace` to witness `RestepTrace` records the shuffle principle for independent trace progressions.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Shuffling Trace

Many trace acts commute: [ReheadTrace](../rehead-trace.md) reorders against
[RetailTrace](../retail-trace.md), [RestepTrace](../restep-trace.md) reorders against
[TraceStep](../trace-step.md), and each head/tail act commutes with the earlier
progressions exposed by [CompleteTrace](../complete-trace.md).  No recognition yet
packages these commutations into a single principle, so we force the renewed
head to witness the stepping remainder: `(ReheadTrace RestepTrace)` earns
***ShuffleTrace***.

`ReheadTrace` and `RestepTrace` already demonstrate the two independent ways a
trace can progress: renewing the head or renewing the remainder.  Their
composition shows these progressions can be swapped without violating any
earned admissibility.  Thus `ShuffleTrace` records that the family of
trace-progressing acts forms a free shuffle algebra—any ordering built from
these steps is admissible, and admissibility is closed under permutation of
independent steps.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Earning Something
act: Something
slug: something
top: Reflex
bottom: Witness
summary: "Testifying to relation still leaves unanswered the question \u201Cwhat carries the testimony?\u201D\u2014we point reflex at that testimony to produce \u201Csomething.\u201D"
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Earning Something

## Interpretation

Testifying to relation still leaves unanswered the question “what carries the testimony?”—we point reflex at that testimony to produce “something.”

Since *Witness* is established, the composition `(Reflex Witness)` applies reflex to the testimony itself.  This self-application records that an act exists which bears the testimony, so we designate the composite as *Something*.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Sounding Fixed Physics Geometry

`SoundProfileGeometry = (PhysicsProfile GeometryProfile)` already records that
profile physics respects the profile geometry.
`FixedGeometryProfile` restricts the global geometry to the fixed recognitions,
recognitions, inheriting the commutation laws between flow and nucleus.
Composing them:
`(SoundProfileGeometry FixedGeometryProfile)` earns
***SoundFixedPhysicsGeometry***,
recording that fixed-physical behaviours remain inside the geometric invariants
they are supposed to respect.

# Sound Physics Semantics

Profile physics is already sound in profile semantics via
[SoundProfilePhysics](../sound-profile-physics.md).
[FixedProfileSemantics](../fixed-profile-semantics.md) restricts those semantics
to the fixed recognitions.  Composing them,
`(SoundProfilePhysics FixedProfileSemantics)`, earns
***SoundFixedPhysicsSemantics***,
ensuring physical judgements remain valid inside the fixed semantics.

# Physics Sounding

## Interpretation

The physical semantics must respect the profile’s judgements.  *Physics Profile* witnessing *Judge Profile* produces ***Sound Physics*** ([SoundPhysics](../sound-physics.md)).

This act records that every judged act remains valid under the declared physics, so *Sound Physics* is established.

# Sound Profile Geometry

`GeometryProfile = (FlowProfile NucleateProfile)` packages the compatible
flow–nucleus pair on the active profile.  `PhysicsProfile = (FlowObservation
FlowProfile)` couples the same flow with observations.  To record that the
physical dynamics respect the geometric invariants, we compose them:
`(PhysicsProfile GeometryProfile)` earns
***SoundProfileGeometry***.

# Sound Profile Physics

`PhysicsProfile = (FlowObservation FlowProfile)` couples the profile flow with
observations to produce physical judgements.
[ProfileSemantics](../profile-semantics.md) interprets those judgements.  Their
composition,
`(PhysicsProfile ProfileSemantics)`, earns
***SoundProfilePhysics***,
recording that profile physics stays inside the profile semantics.

title: Spectral Geometry
act: SpectralGeometry
slug: spectral-geometry
top: WaveOperator
bottom: Laplacian
summary: Combine the Laplacian and wave operator to build the internal spectral calculus.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Spectral Geometry

Spectral geometry studies spaces through eigenvalues and eigenvectors of
operators like Laplacians and heat kernels.  We package the Laplacian, heat
kernel, and wave operator by composing the wave operator with the Laplacian:
`(WaveOperator Laplacian)` earns ***SpectralGeometry***.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Spectrum Map
act: SpectrumMap
slug: spectrum-map
top: FixedNucleateProfile
bottom: SpectrumSeed
summary: Collapse the spectral seed via the fixed nucleus to extract the stable eigencomponent.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Spectrum Map

The spectral map extracts eigenvalues/eigenshapes by comparing the seed to the
mode.  We use the fixed nucleus to collapse their difference, composing
[FixedNucleateProfile](../fixed-nucleate-profile.md) with
[SpectrumSeed](../spectrum-seed.md):
`(FixedNucleateProfile SpectrumSeed)` earns
***SpectrumMap***.

`SpectrumSeed` records the kernel’s action on harmonics, and
`FixedNucleateProfile` collapses recognitions under the fixed closure.  Their
composition isolates the stable part of that action—the eigencomponent—yielding
the spectrum map.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Spectrum Seed
act: SpectrumSeed
slug: spectrum-seed
top: ObservationKernel
bottom: HarmonicMode
summary: Seed the spectrum by letting the observation kernel act on a harmonic mode.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Spectrum Seed

The spectrum of an operator measures its action on harmonic modes.  To seed the
spectrum we let the observation kernel act on a harmonic mode:
`(ObservationKernel HarmonicMode)` earns ***SpectrumSeed***.

[ObservationKernel](../2026/03/04/2026-03-04-observation-kernel.md) evolves states and attaches
observational distributions, while [HarmonicMode](../harmonic-mode.md)
is a stable oscillatory pattern.  Their composition records how the kernel
acts on this harmonic, yielding a spectral seed.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Spectrum
act: Spectrum
slug: spectrum
top: TraceProfile
bottom: SpectrumMap
summary: Evaluate the spectral map across the profile trace by composing `TraceProfile` with `SpectrumMap`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Spectrum

A full spectrum requires evaluating the spectral map across the trace of a
profile, so we let [TraceProfile](../trace-profile.md) witness
[SpectrumMap](../spectrum-map.md):
`(TraceProfile SpectrumMap)` earns ***Spectrum***.

`SpectrumMap` contains eigencomponents of the observation kernel, and
`TraceProfile` threads these through all profile recognitions.  Their
composition produces the spectrum of the profile.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

title: Stabilizing Context

slug: stabilize-context
top: RecognizeContext
bottom: StabilizeJudgement
summary: '*Stabilize Judgement* shows contexts only matter through the stable judgements they support, but we must identify contexts giving the same stable judgements to all stabilized terms. *Recognize Context* witnessing *Stabilize Judgement* produces ***Stabilize Context*** (`StabilizeContext`).'
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Stabilizing Context

## Interpretation

*Stabilize Judgement* shows contexts only matter through the stable judgements they support, but we must identify contexts giving the same stable judgements to all stabilized terms.  *Recognize Context* witnessing *Stabilize Judgement* produces ***Stabilize Context*** ([StabilizeContext](../stabilize-context.md)).

The composition uses previously earned recognitions and identifies such contexts, so *Stabilize Context* follows.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}

# Maintaining Invariant

## Interpretation

*Flow* fixed points that remain states represent invariants.  *Fix Flow* witnessing *Physics State* produces ***Stabilize Invariant*** ([StabilizeInvariant](../stabilize-invariant.md)).

This composition uses earlier recognitions and records precisely those states fixed by the flow, so *Stabilize Invariant* follows.

# Stabilizing Judgement

## Interpretation

*Close Judgement Trace* shows that observationally aligned triadic judgements close, yet we still lack the identification collapsing judgements with the same closed behaviour.  Forcing *Recognize Judgement Triad* to witness *Close Judgement Trace* yields ***Stabilize Judgement*** ([StabilizeJudgement](../stabilize-judgement.md)).

This composition identifies triadic judgements that close in the same way, so *Stabilize Judgement* follows directly from earned recognitions.

# Stabilizing Observation

## Interpretation

*Normalize Observation Equivalence* ensures normalized observations agree across all contexts, yet we must identify any two such observations.  *Recognize Observation* witnessing *Normalize Observation Equivalence* yields ***Stabilize Observation*** ([StabilizeObservation](../stabilize-observation.md)).

This composition collapses observationally equivalent normalized observations, so *Stabilize Observation* is valid.

---
# Stabilizing Term

## Interpretation

*Equate Contexts* tells us when two terms are indistinguishable to every recognized context, but we must quotient those terms.  Letting *Seed Term* witness *Equate Contexts* produces ***Stabilize Term*** ([StabilizeTerm](../stabilize-term.md)).

By composing an earned seed with the equivalence, we identify contextually indistinguishable terms, so *Stabilize Term* is established.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Stabilizing Trace
act: StabilizeTrace
slug: stabilize-trace
top: CompleteTrace
bottom: TraceObservationEquivalenceClosure
summary: '*Trace Observation Equivalence Closure* ensures that observation-equivalent traces close, but it does not yet identify traces with identical closed behaviour. Letting *Complete Trace* witness *Trace Observation Equivalence Closure* produces ***Stabilize Trace*** (`StabilizeTrace`).'
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Stabilizing Trace

## Interpretation

*Trace Observation Equivalence Closure* ensures that observation-equivalent traces close, but it does not yet identify traces with identical closed behaviour.  Letting *Complete Trace* witness *Trace Observation Equivalence Closure* produces ***Stabilize Trace*** ([StabilizeTrace](../stabilize-trace.md)).

Composing the whole trace with its closure identifies any two traces that share the same closed observation acts, so *Stabilize Trace* is earned using only prior recognitions.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Starting Trace

## Interpretation

With leading and trailing orientations established, letting *Lead Entry* witness *Trail Entry* initiates the recorded trace.

The tree `(LeadEntry TrailEntry)` states that the act *Lead Entry* witnesses the act *Trail Entry*.  This first oriented pairing marks the beginning of the trace, hence the name *Start Trace*.

# Tracing Context

## Interpretation

Having recognized contexts, we still lack the trace that records how arbitrary traces become contexts.  *Contextualize Trace* must therefore witness *Recognize Context*, yielding ***Trace Context*** ([TraceContext](../trace-context.md)).

This act threads recognized contexts back through the trace: `(ContextualizeTrace RecognizeContext)` represents the named trace observing the recognition.  The resulting composite is the promised *Trace Context*.

---
title: Transition Geometry
act: TransitionGeometry
slug: transition-geometry
top: FixedInvariantLattice
bottom: TransitionLaw
summary: Study how the transition law acts on the invariant lattice by letting `FixedInvariantLattice` witness `TransitionLaw`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Transition Geometry

The fixed invariant lattice describes the order–meet–join structure of
stable states, and the observation kernel sends each state into a successor
distribution.  To observe how the kernel acts geometrically, we let the
invariant lattice witness the transition law:
`(FixedInvariantLattice TransitionLaw)` earns
***TransitionGeometry***.

[FixedInvariantLattice](../fixed-invariant-lattice.md) organises fixed invariants
by meet–join structure, and [TransitionLaw](../transition-law.md)
provides the relational successor step.  Their composition records how the
successor relation respects the lattice—whether transitions go up, down, or
sideways in the partial order—yielding the geometric view of the kernel
structure.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

{{ act_nav(page.meta) }}
---
title: Transition Law
act: TransitionLaw
slug: transition-law
top: FlowMeasureStep
bottom: FixedInvariant
summary: Map fixed invariant states through the one-step update by composing `FlowMeasureStep` with `FixedInvariant`.
tags:
- act
layer: act
status: plan
---
# Transition Law

A transition law records how each invariant state spreads into its possible
successors.  With the one-step update
([FlowMeasureStep](../flow-measure-step.md)) and the domain of fixed invariant
states ([FixedInvariant](../fixed-invariant.md)), we let the step witness
the invariant recognitions to obtain the transition map:
`(FlowMeasureStep FixedInvariant)` earns ***TransitionLaw***.

`FixedInvariant` lists the fixed states, and `FlowMeasureStep`
describes how a step transforms any such state.  Their composition maps
each fixed invariant into its evolution–measurement successors, yielding the
transition law of the observation kernel.


{{ act_info(page.meta) }}

# Folding Void

## Interpretation

Nothing shows that *Witness* observes *Something* in two symmetric positions; folding that symmetry back on itself produces the void.

The trace `(Nothing Nothing)` reuses *Nothing* as both witness and object.  This self-application forces the symmetry between the two appearances of *Witness* and *Something* to collapse, yielding a closed loop that we name *Void*.

# Adjunction Counit

Counit natural transformation for the flow–nucleus adjunction.

## Definition
- Inputs: a [Context](../../../../structure/context.md).
- Outputs: counit map `epsilon: flow ∘ nucleus → Id` at that context.

## Invariants
- Natural in context.
- Satisfies the other triangle identity with the unit.

## Proof
- Uses: [Flow–Nucleus Adjunction](../flow-nucleus-adjunction.md) to
  supply the counit.
- Shows that: counit maps exist and satisfy the adjunction laws.

title: Base-Change Square
kind: operator
layer: math
status: plan
uses:
  - math/structure/relational-sheaf-topos
  - math/structure/base-change-condition
  - math/structure/subobject-classifier
  - math/structure/recognition-classifier
produces:
  - math/structure/base-change-condition
used_by: []
produced_by: []
---
# Base-Change Square

Verifies the Base-Change condition on a given pullback square: pullback and
pushforward commute on subobjects/recognitions.

## Definition
- Inputs: a pullback square in the [Relational Sheaf Topos](../../../../structure/relational-sheaf-topos.md),
  and subobjects expressed via [Subobject Classifier](../../../../structure/subobject-classifier.md).
- Outputs: a [Base-Change Condition](../base-change-condition.md)
  witness for that square.
- Behaviour: constructs characteristic maps, applies pullback/pushforward, and
  checks that the two routes coincide.

## Invariants
- Commutation: pullback ∘ pushforward = pushforward ∘ pullback for the square.
- Logical stability: characteristic maps are natural under base change.

## Proof
- Uses: internal pullbacks/pushforwards in the relational sheaf topos and the
  subobject classifier to express subobjects.
- Shows that: the given square satisfies Base-Change, yielding the condition
  witness used for substitution and logical relations.
---
title: Baseline Convergence Test
kind: operator
layer: math
status: plan
derived_from: acts/baseline-convergence
uses:
  - math/structure/baseline-remainder
produces:
  - math/structure/baseline-remainder
used_by: []
produced_by: []
---
# Baseline Convergence Test

## Statement

The act [`baseline-convergence`](../baseline-convergence.md) witnesses a
test that detects exactly when a trace tail collapses to the void.

## Definition
- Inputs: [Baseline Remainder](../baseline-remainder.md), [Step Trace](../step-trace.md) context.
- Outputs: updated [Baseline Remainder](../baseline-remainder.md) indicating collapse to baseline.
- Behaviour: applies the act [Baseline Convergence](../baseline-convergence.md) to check iterative reflex steps on a trace tail; if the iteration stabilizes against `Nothing`, the tail collapses to the baseline.

## Invariants
- Detects collapse without altering the trace tail unless convergence is witnessed.
- Resulting baseline remainder matches the earned convergence behaviour (indistinguishable from baseline).

## Proof
- Derived from: [Baseline Convergence](../baseline-convergence.md).
- Shows that: comparing iterative reflex steps against `Nothing` on a trace tail yields a baseline remainder exactly when the tail collapses; uniqueness up to earned equivalence follows from the act.

Given the earned trace relation and the convergence act, this test is fixed up
to nucleus equivalence.
---
title: Baseline Limit Extraction
tags:
  - trace
  - baseline
layer: internal-theorem
status: plan
depends_on:
  - acts/baseline-limit
  - acts/baseline-convergence
  - acts/tail-trace
  - math/structure/baseline-remainder
claims:
  - type: operator
    judgement: BaselineLimit isolates the stabilized tail BaselineRemainder
---
# Baseline Limit Extraction

## Statement

The act [`baseline-limit`](../baseline-limit.md) applies the convergence
test to [`tail-trace`](../tail-trace.md) to isolate the stabilized tail
`BaselineRemainder`.

## Proof sketch

`BaselineLimit = (BaselineConvergence TailTrace)` records that once convergence
is known, watching the tail through it isolates the exact remainder. Any other
observation factors through this composite, so the limit is canonical.

## Uniqueness

Because `BaselineRemainder` is earned by these acts, limit extraction is fixed
up to nucleus equivalence.

# Characteristic Map

Constructs the characteristic map of a subobject into the recognition
classifier (Omega) in the relational sheaf topos.

## Definition
- Inputs: a monomorphism/subobject in the relational sheaf topos, expressed via
  the [Subobject Classifier](../../../../structure/subobject-classifier.md).
- Outputs: its characteristic morphism into the
  [Recognition Classifier](../recognition-classifier.md).
- Behaviour: assigns to each element/section the recognition (truth value)
  indicating membership in the subobject.

## Invariants
- Classifier property: the subobject is the pullback of `true` along the
  characteristic map.
- Naturality: characteristic maps commute with pullbacks (Base-Change).

## Proof
- Uses: [Subobject Classifier](../../../../structure/subobject-classifier.md) and
  [Recognition Classifier](../recognition-classifier.md) to define
  characteristic maps.
- Shows that: every subobject admits a unique characteristic map satisfying the
  classifier property, stable under base change.
---

The **Check Ergodicity** operator witnesses ergodicity for the fixed kernel,
equating time and space averages.

  [Mixing Property](../mixing-property.md).
- Outputs: [Ergodicity Property](../ergodicity-property.md) and the
  same fixed kernel.
- Behaviour: checks that mixing implies ergodicity: time averages equal space
  averages for observables under this kernel.

## Invariants
- Confirms mixing implies ergodicity for the fixed kernel.
- Keeps the kernel within its earned carrier.

## Proof
Uses the [Ergodicity](../../../../acts/ergodicity.md) act applied to a fixed kernel with
mixing property to yield the ergodicity property. Shows that ergodicity is
earned without leaving the fixed-kernel carrier.

title: Check Parametric Uniformity

# Check Parametric Uniformity

The **Check Parametric Uniformity** operator asserts the parametricity
property: terms related in one indexed context remain related under all
reindexings of the context geometry.

## Definition
- Inputs: an [Indexed Category](../indexed-category.md) over the
  [Index Base](../index-base.md) and its corresponding
  [Parametric Semantics](../parametric-semantics.md) carrier.
- Behaviour: checks, using the [Context Adjunction Witness](../context-adjunction-witness.md),
  that reindexing along any base morphism preserves the parametric semantic
  relations.
- Outputs: the same [Parametric Semantics](../parametric-semantics.md)
  equipped with a uniformity witness.

## Invariants
- Reindexing respects the flow–nucleus adjunction.
- Related arguments remain related under all reindexings in the index base.

## Proof
- Derived from: [Context Adjunction Witness](../context-adjunction-witness.md),
  which supplies the unit/counit equations for reindexing.
- Uses: [Indexed Category](../indexed-category.md) to transport
  contexts and [Parametric Semantics](../parametric-semantics.md) as
  the relations being preserved.
- Shows that: when reindexing is governed by the adjunction witness, the
  parametric semantics remain invariant across base morphisms, yielding the
  uniformity certificate.
---

# Classify Subobject

Constructs characteristic maps for subobjects using the recognition algebra
and the subobject classifier.

## Definition
- Inputs: a mono `m: A → B` in the earned topos; the
  [Relational Logic Algebra](../relational-logic-algebra.md) and
  [Subobject Classifier](../../../../structure/subobject-classifier.md).
- Outputs: characteristic map `chi_m: B → Omega` exhibiting `A` as the
  pullback of `true`.
- Behaviour: map elements/sections of `B` to recognitions indicating membership
  in the subobject; encode as `chi_m` into `Omega`.

## Invariants
- Correctness: `chi_m` satisfies the classifier property (pullback square with
  `true` recovers `A`).
- Naturality: characteristic maps are stable under pullback.
- Logical alignment: `chi_m` respects the logical operations of the recognition
  algebra.

## Proof
- Uses: [Relational Logic Algebra](../relational-logic-algebra.md)
  to interpret membership as recognition; [Subobject Classifier](../../../../structure/subobject-classifier.md)
  to target characteristic maps.
- Shows that: characteristic maps constructed from recognitions satisfy the
  subobject classifier axioms, enabling classification of subobjects by
  earned recognitions.
---

# Compose Frame

Partial composition `compose : Frame × Frame ⇀ Frame` for frames from
[`EvaluationContext`](../evaluation-context.md).

## Statement

Whenever `compose(F1, F2)` is defined,

```
plug(compose(F1, F2), t) = plug(F1, plug(F2, t))
```

and composition is associative where defined, with `empty` as neutral.

## Proof

Sketch: define `compose` by nesting holes along the trace spine, using frames
generated by [`BuildEvaluationContext`](../build-evaluation-context.md).
Show associativity and neutrality of `empty` from
[`SeedEvaluationContext`](../seed-evaluation-context.md) by
structural induction on frames and the definition of `plug`.
---

# Context Adjunction

Adjunction between context flow and context nucleus, earned by
`ContextAdjunctionWitness` and `DynamicResiduation`.

## Statement

Natural bijection for contexts `C, D`:

```
Hom(flow(C), D) ≅ Hom(C, nucleus(D))
```

## Proof

Sketch: `DynamicResiduation` provides the hom-redistribution; `ContextFlow` and
`ContextNucleus` are the endofunctors. The bijection yields the adjunction,
with unit/counit witnessing the monad/comonad induced by the adjunction.

# Evaluation Normalisation

## Statement

There exists a partial operator

```text
norm : EvalState ⇀ EvalState
```

that selects the normal state reached by iterating `step` when strong
normalization applies.

## Definition

- Iterates `step` from an initial state; by strong normalization, a normal
  state is reached when the program is in the earned fragment.
- By confluence, this normal state is unique up to earned equivalence; `norm`
  selects it.

## Uniqueness

With `EvalState`, `step`, strong normalization, and confluence fixed, `norm` is
unique in the earned sense.

title: Flow–Normalization Compatibility
tags:
  - flow
  - normalization
  - equivalence
layer: internal-theorem
status: plan
depends_on:
  - math/operator/flow-nucleus-commutation
  - theorem/internal/admissible-normal-form-structure
  - acts/flow
claims:
  - type: operator
    judgement: For admissible morphisms, flow commutes with normalization up to nucleus equivalence
---
# Flow–Normalization Compatibility

## Statement

On a stratified ∞-site with admissible morphisms `Adm` and normal-form map
`N∞ : Adm → Adm`, any earned flow `F_t` (compatible with stratification,
coverage, and admissibility) commutes with normalization up to nucleus
equivalence:

```text
F_t ∘ N∞ ≃ N∞ ∘ F_t on Adm
```

## Proof sketch

- By flow–nucleus compatibility, `F_t` preserves nucleus classes on admissible
  morphisms and respects restriction/gluing.
- By the normal-form structure, `N∞` picks a canonical representative in each
  rewrite class.
- Applying `F_t` then `N∞`, or `N∞` then `F_t`, yields nucleus-equivalent
  morphisms because `F_t` cannot leave the admissible/coverage constraints and
  normalization collapses within each flow orbit.

## Uniqueness

Given the earned flow–nucleus compatibility and the normal-form map, this
commutation property is fixed in the earned sense.
---

# Flow–Nucleus Commutation

## Statement

Flow and nucleus commute on admissible morphisms: applying the nucleus before
or after flow yields the same stabilised recognition. The acts also witness the
adjunction between flow and nucleus on admissible morphisms.

## Definition
- Inputs: [Admissible Morphism](../admissible-morphism.md).
- Outputs: commutation/adjunction witness (law) for flow/nucleus on admissible morphisms.
- Behaviour: enforces that `N ∘ F = F ∘ N` on admissible morphisms and that flow/nucleus form an adjoint pair, per the earned acts.

## Invariants
- Commutation holds: flow and nucleus can be reordered on admissible morphisms without changing the result.
- Adjunction holds: flow is left adjoint to nucleus on admissible morphisms, as witnessed by the earned acts.

## Proof
- Derived from: [Commute Flow and Nucleus](../commute-flow-and-nucleus.md).
- Uses: [Admissible Morphism](../admissible-morphism.md) to scope the commutation/adjunction.
- Shows that: the commutation and adjunction laws earned by the acts apply to admissible morphisms, fixing the flow–nucleus interaction up to earned equivalence.

Given these acts, the flow–nucleus commutation/adjunction is fixed up to earned
equivalence.
---

# Fundamental Reducibility

## Statement

The act [`establish-fundamental-reducibility`](../establish-fundamental-reducibility.md)
earns that every well-typed term is reducible in every reducible environment,
closing the logical-relations argument.

## Proof sketch

`SelectReducibilityCandidate` witnessing `SubstituteReducibility` is the
standard logical-relations argument; the act records that every well-typed term
lies in the reducibility relation in any `ReducibleEnvironment`.

## Uniqueness

Because the reducibility relation and environment are fixed by the earned acts,
this fundamental property is unique in the earned sense.

## Proof
- Derived from: [Establish Fundamental Reducibility](../establish-fundamental-reducibility.md).

# Load Persistent Context

## Statement

There exists an operator

```text
load_context : (PersistentContext, BaselineRemainder, PersistentRecognitionCarrier) ⇀ PersistentContextCarrier
```

that replays a stored persistent context (and its fixed recognitions) to yield
an earned persistent context up to nucleus equivalence.

## Definition by acts

- Uses [`retrace-context`](../retrace-context.md) to replay the stored
  context on a trace.
- Uses [`renew-context`](../renew-context.md) to reintroduce the baseline
  remainder.
- Reattaches fixed recognitions from `PersistentRecognitionCarrier`.

## Uniqueness

Because the inputs are earned carriers and the replay/renew acts fix the
behaviour, any operator respecting these produces a nucleus-equivalent
persistent context; thus `load_context` is unique in the earned sense.
---

used_by:
  - math/structure/loop-space

# Path Compose

Concatenates directed paths when endpoints match.

## Definition
- Inputs: paths `p: x → y`, `q: y → z` in [Directed Path](../directed-path.md).
- Output: concatenated path `q ∘ p: x → z` modulo homotopy.

## Invariants
- Unital: composing with identity path yields the same class up to homotopy.
- Associative up to homotopy (associator earned in the directed path/groupoid
  structure).
- Directed: only composable when endpoints match in order.

## Proof
Uses the directed-path structure to define concatenation and the groupoid data
to supply associator/unit homotopies. Shows that: concatenation yields a
well-defined morphism in the directed path/fundamental groupoid.
---

# Identify Path

Provides the identity (constant) path at a recognition/context.

## Definition
- Inputs: a point/recognition `x` in the
  [Recognition Carrier](../recognition-carrier.md).
- Outputs: identity path `id_x: x → x` in
  [Directed Path](../directed-path.md).

## Invariants
- Unit: for any path `p: x → y`, `p ∘ id_x = p` and `id_y ∘ p = p` up to
  homotopy; unitors established in the directed path/groupoid structure.

## Proof
Uses the directed-path structure to form constant paths and the groupoid data
to supply unit homotopies. Shows that identity paths act as units for path
composition in the directed path/fundamental groupoid.
---

title: Recognition Implication
kind: operator

status: plan

uses:
  - math/structure/recognition-carrier
produces:
  - math/structure/recognition-carrier
used_by: []
produced_by: []
---
# Recognition Implication

Implements implication on earned recognitions as the right adjoint to meet.

## Definition
- Inputs: two recognitions in the [Recognition Carrier](../recognition-carrier.md).
- Outputs: the implication recognition `a ⇒ b`.
- Behaviour: yields the greatest recognition `x` such that `a ∧ x ≤ b`.

## Invariants
- Adjunction: `a ∧ c ≤ b` iff `c ≤ (a ⇒ b)`.
- Monotone in the consequent, antitone in the antecedent.

## Proof
- Derived from: [Imply Recognition](../imply-recognition.md).
- Shows that: implication is the right adjoint to meet in the recognition
  lattice, satisfying implication laws.
---

used_by: []

# Recognition Join

Implements the binary join (disjunction) on earned recognitions.

## Definition
- Inputs: two recognitions in the [Recognition Carrier](../recognition-carrier.md).
- Outputs: their join recognition.
- Behaviour: yields the least recognition above both inputs in the entailment
  order.

## Invariants
- Commutative, associative, idempotent.
- Monotone: if `a ≤ a'` and `b ≤ b'` then `a ∨ b ≤ a' ∨ b'`.

## Proof
- Derived from: [Join Recognition](../join-recognition.md).
- Shows that: the join act yields a well-defined binary operation on
  recognitions satisfying the lattice laws.
---

- Inputs: two recognitions in the [Recognition Carrier](../recognition-carrier.md).
- Outputs: their meet recognition.
- Behaviour: yields the greatest recognition below both inputs in the entailment
  order.

## Invariants
- Commutative, associative, idempotent.
- Monotone: if `a ≤ a'` and `b ≤ b'` then `a ∧ b ≤ a' ∧ b'`.

## Proof

- Shows that: the meet act yields a well-defined binary operation on
  recognitions satisfying the lattice laws.

produces:

# Recognition Pseudocomplement

Implements Negate on earned recognitions: the pseudocomplement.

## Definition
- Inputs: a recognition in the [Recognition Carrier](../recognition-carrier.md).
- Outputs: its pseudocomplement `¬a := (a ⇒ ⊥)`.
- Behaviour: yields the largest recognition whose meet with `a` is bottom.

## Invariants
- `a ∧ ¬a = ⊥` and if `a ∧ x = ⊥` then `x ≤ ¬a`.
- Monotone: if `a ≤ b` then `¬b ≤ ¬a`.

## Proof
- Derived from: [Negate](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/math-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/complement.md).
- Shows that: Negate is the pseudocomplement operation in the recognition
  lattice, consistent with Heyting algebra laws.
---

# Reducible Substitution

## Statement

The earned acts
[`select-reducibility-candidate`](../select-reducibility-candidate.md) and
[`substitute-reducibility`](../substitute-reducibility.md) show that
substituting typed reducible terms into a `ReducibleEnvironment` preserves
reducibility.

## Proof sketch

`BuildReducibleEnvironment` seeds the logical relation. The selection act
chooses the candidate relation for each term, and
`SubstituteReducibility = (BuildReducibleEnvironment JudgeTyping)` certifies
that typed substitutions stay reducible.

## Uniqueness

Given the earned environment and substitution acts, reducible substitution is
fixed up to nucleus equivalence.
---
title: Reindexing
kind: operator
layer: math
status: plan
derived_from: acts/substitute-reducibility
uses: []
produces: []
---
# Reindexing

## Definition
- Inputs: a morphism in the [Index Base](../index-base.md) and a judgemental object in the source [Fiber Category](../fiber-category.md).
- Outputs: the pullback of that judgement along the base morphism as a judgemental object in the target fibre.
- Behaviour: restricts substitution along a context morphism, transporting recognised terms, types, and judgements while preserving the typing and entailment guarantees earned by the substitution reducibility act.

## Invariants
- Functorial: pulling back along identity morphisms is identity on fibres; pullbacks along composed morphisms compose accordingly.
- Type- and entailment-preserving: transported judgements remain well-typed and maintain the reducibility guarantees from [SubstituteReducibility](../substitute-reducibility.md).

## Proof
- Derived from: [SubstituteReducibility](../substitute-reducibility.md), which shows substitution preserves typing and entailment.
- Shows that: restricting substitution along a base morphism defines a functor between fibre categories, satisfying identity and composition laws and preserving recognised judgemental structure.
---

# Glue Sheaf

Glues a matching family of sections over a cover of windows into a unique
global section, enforcing the sheaf condition.

## Definition
- Inputs:
  - A cover of a window by sub-windows in [Hypertensor Window](../hypertensor-window.md).
  - A matching family of sections in [Hypertensor Section](../hypertensor-section.md)
    agreeing on overlaps.
- Output: a unique global section over the covered window in
  [Hypertensor Section](../hypertensor-section.md).
- Behaviour:
  1. Check matching on pairwise overlaps.
  2. Construct the amalgamated section.
  3. Verify uniqueness up to earned equivalence.

## Invariants
- Locality: if two glued sections agree on all sub-windows of a cover, they are
  equal.
- Gluing: every matching family admits a unique amalgamation.
- Compatibility: respects restriction maps of the [Sheaf](../../../../structure/sheaf.md).

## Proof
- Uses: [Hypertensor Sheaf](../hypertensor-sheaf.md) and
  [Sheaf](../../../../structure/sheaf.md) structures to provide restriction/gluing data.
- Shows that: the earned sheaf condition holds by producing unique amalgams
  from matching families over covers.
---

# Storage Load Operator

## Statement

There exists an operator

```text
load_snapshot : SnapId ⇀ Section
```

that retrieves `SnapContent` for a given `SnapId` and reconstructs a sheaf
`Section` nucleus-equivalent to the stored one.

## Definition by acts

- Given `SnapId`, retrieve its `SnapContent` (observations, baseline remainder,
  fixed recognitions).
- Use [`retrace-context`](../retrace-context.md) to replay the stored
  context and [`renew-context`](../renew-context.md) to reintroduce the
  baseline remainder and fixed recognitions.
- Produce a `Section` nucleus-equivalent to the one that was saved.

## Uniqueness

Because `SnapContent` is fixed by the earned carriers and retrace/renew acts
determine reconstruction, any operator respecting these produces the same
`Section` up to nucleus equivalence; `load_snapshot` is unique in the earned
sense.
---

# Storage Save Operator

## Statement

There exists an operator

```text
save_snapshot : Section → (SnapContent, SnapId)
```

that takes a sheaf `Section` and produces snapshot content paired with a fresh
`SnapId`.

## Definition by acts

- From a `Section`, extract observations, the baseline remainder via
  [`baseline-limit`](../baseline-limit.md), and fixed recognitions eligible
  for storage (`PersistentRecognitionCarrier`).
- Package these into `SnapContent` (see
  [`storage-snapshot-carrier`](../storage-snapshot-carrier.md)).
- Allocate a fresh `SnapId` and return `(SnapContent, SnapId)`.

## Uniqueness

Because `SnapContent` and `SnapId` are fixed by the earned carriers and the
freshness discipline, any operator respecting these yields the same result up
to nucleus equivalence; `save_snapshot` is unique in the earned sense.
---

# Strong Normalization

## Statement

The earned acts [`normalization`](../../../../acts/normalization.md),
[`step-trace`](../step-trace.md), and
[`normalize-strongly`](../normalize-strongly.md) jointly earn that every
well-founded reduction trace collapses to normal form.

## Proof sketch

`Normalization` applied to `StepTrace` collapses any infinite tail; the act
`NormalizeStrongly` records that collapse explicitly, so every admissible
reduction terminates.

## Uniqueness

Because the normalization acts fix the reduction relation and its collapse,
this strong-normalization property is unique in the earned sense.

## Proof
- Derived from: [Normalize Strongly](../normalize-strongly.md).
- Inputs: stabilization of reduction traces via [Trace Renewal](../../../../../../../../../engineering-science-services-section/services/computer-science-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/trace-renewal.md).
- Shows that: every admissible reduction trace collapses to normal form.

[`substitution-term`](../substitution-term.md), and

Substitute : Term × Term × Context ⇀ Term

that is well-defined on the earned term grammar and preserves reducibility.

## Properties

- Well-defined on earned terms/contexts (`seed-term`, `substitution-term`).
- Preserves reducibility predicates used in normalization (`substitute-reducibility`).
- Interacts coherently with evaluation contexts built from the evaluation
  context structure.

## Uniqueness

Given these acts, the substitution discipline is fixed up to nucleus
equivalence.

# Trace Renewal

## Statement

The acts [`renew-trace`](../renew-trace.md),
[`restep-trace`](../restep-trace.md), and
[`iterative-persistence`](../iterative-persistence.md) earn re-entry of a
stabilized tail into the trace without altering its baseline remainder.

## Proof sketch

`RenewTrace` rethreads the remainder into the head of the trace. `RestepTrace`
witnesses that after renewal the tail matches the previous limit.
`IterativePersistence` guarantees this behaviour persists under further
iterations, so renewal does not disturb the limit.

## Uniqueness

Given these earned acts, the renewal behaviour is fixed up to nucleus
equivalence.

## Proof
- Derived from: [Renew Trace](../renew-trace.md).
- Shows that: renewing and re-stepping traces leaves the baseline remainder unchanged under iterative persistence.

title: Trace Shuffle Count
tags:
  - trace
  - shuffle
  - combinatorics
layer: internal-theorem
status: plan
depends_on:
  - math/structure/trace-shuffle-class
claims:
  - type: operator
    judgement: |Reorder(T_n)| satisfies the Fibonacci recurrence
---
# Trace Shuffle Count

## Statement

The sizes of shuffle classes satisfy

```text
|Reorder(T_0)| = 1, |Reorder(T_1)| = 1,
|Reorder(T_n)| = |Reorder(T_{n-1})| + |Reorder(T_{n-2})| for n ≥ 2
```

so `|Reorder(T_n)| = F_n`.

## Proof sketch

Decomposition and free shuffle imply that `Reorder(T_n)` splits into reorderings
beginning with a `T_{n-1}` progress and those beginning with a `T_{n-2}`
progress, yielding the Fibonacci recurrence.

## Uniqueness

Given the earned shuffle classes, the count is determined.
---

normalization and reducibility acts.

- Rewrite relation `⇒` on `Adm` generated by eta/beta/tau-style steps from the
  normalization acts.
- Well-founded measure on `Adm` ensuring termination of `⇒`.
- Confluence up to coherent cells, yielding unique normal forms in the 1-truncation.
- Normal-form map `N∞ : Adm → Adm` sending each admissible morphism to its
  canonical normal representative.

## Proof

Sketch: use `NormalizeStrongly`, `NormalizeSyntax`, and `SummarizeReduction` to
define the rewrite relation on `Adm` (eta/beta/tau steps). Show termination via
the earned measure and confluence via critical-pair joins, yielding unique
normal forms. Define `N∞` accordingly.

commute, giving substitution stability for recognitions/subobjects.

  the pullback of a pushforward equals the pushforward of a pullback.

  being natural with respect to base change.

## Invariants

- Naturality: substitution along base change preserves logical structure.
- Compatibility: holds for recognitions/subobjects across the topos.

- Derived from: [Relational Sheaf Topos](../2026/03/04/2026-03-04-relational-sheaf-topos.md) with
  stable pullbacks/pushforwards.

  [Subobject Classifier](../../../../triage/subobject-classifier.md) to express subobjects and
  their characteristic maps.

  stability for recognitions/logical predicates.

this yields the usual detailed-balance condition and preserves the earned
observation kernel and fixed-state carriers.

title: Entropy Spectral Fixed Point
kind: structure
layer: math
status: plan
uses:
  - math/structure/mixing-property
  - math/structure/ergodicity-property
  - math/structure/entropy-functional
  - math/structure/hypertensor-stochastic-evolution

  - math/structure/profile-kernel
  - math/structure/harmonic-mode

  - math/structure/fixed-kernel

produced_by: []
---
# Entropy Spectral Fixed Point

Theorem-level structure stating that entropy along hypertensor Stochastic evolution
is monotone and converges to a spectral fixed point determined by the leading
harmonic mode/eigen-spectrum of the observation/profile kernels.

## Statement
- Context: [Hypertensor Stochastic Evolution](../hypertensor-stochastic-evolution.md)
  over [Observation Kernel](../2026/03/04/2026-03-04-observation-kernel.md) / [Profile Kernel](../profile-kernel.md).
- Assumptions (Stochastic setting):
  - Kernels are positive and conserve mass (stochastic/Stochastic).
  - Detailed balance or contraction property for the entropy functional holds.
  - [Mixing](../mixing.md)/[Ergodicity](../../../../acts/ergodicity.md) acts earned.
- Entropy monotonicity: along the evolution the
  [Entropy Functional](../entropy-functional.md) (e.g., relative entropy to the
  invariant measure) is non-increasing; strict decrease holds under the mixing
  hypotheses unless at the invariant state.
- Spectral fixed point: the limit state is the
  [Fixed Kernel](../fixed-kernel.md)/[Fixed Chain](../fixed-chain.md) characterised
  by the leading [Harmonic Mode](../harmonic-mode.md) in the [Spectrum](../spectrum.md).
- Uniqueness: mixing/ergodicity imply a unique
  [Fixed Invariant State](../fixed-invariant-state.md); convergence rate governed
  by the spectral gap (difference between the leading mode and the next).

## Proof sketch
- Monotonicity: [Hypertensor Stochastic Evolution](../hypertensor-stochastic-evolution.md)
  composed with [Entropy Functional](../entropy-functional.md) yields a
  contractive flow (entropy production non-negative); mixing/ergodicity make it
  strictly contractive unless already at a fixed point. The entropy production
  inequality yields `d/dt H ≤ 0`, with equality only at the invariant.
- Spectral convergence: spectral decomposition of the
  [Observation/Profile Kernel](../2026/03/04/2026-03-04-observation-kernel.md) shows higher modes decay
  at rates set by their eigenvalues; the leading [Harmonic Mode](../harmonic-mode.md)
  remains, yielding the [Fixed Kernel/Chain](../fixed-kernel.md)/(fixed-chain.md).
- Uniqueness: [Mixing](../mixing.md) and [Ergodicity](../../../../acts/ergodicity.md)
  acts force a single stationary state; entropy minimum attained only there.

## Proof
- Uses: [Entropy Functional](../entropy-functional.md),
  [Hypertensor Stochastic Evolution](../hypertensor-stochastic-evolution.md),
  [Observation Kernel](../2026/03/04/2026-03-04-observation-kernel.md),
  [Profile Kernel](../profile-kernel.md),
  [Harmonic Mode](../harmonic-mode.md), [Spectrum](../spectrum.md),
  [Fixed Kernel](../fixed-kernel.md), [Fixed Chain](../fixed-chain.md).
- Produces: [Fixed Invariant State](../fixed-invariant-state.md) as the entropy
  minimiser / spectral fixed point.
- Shows that: along hypertensor Stochastic evolution, entropy decreases and the
  state converges to the spectral fixed point; mixing/ergodicity guarantee
  uniqueness and alignment between entropy minimum and leading spectral data.
---

# Ergodicity Property

Structure capturing ergodicity: time averages equal space averages with respect
to the fixed invariant state.

## Definition
- Carrier: predicates tying a [Fixed Invariant State](../fixed-invariant-state.md)
  to long-run averages along the kernel evolution, assuming
  [Mixing Property](../mixing-property.md).
- Data: statements that for any observable, the time average along trajectories
  converges to the invariant-state expectation.

## Invariants
- Uniqueness: ergodicity implies a unique invariant state up to null sets.
- Convergence: time averages converge to expectations for all integrable
  observables.

## Proof
- Derived from: [Ergodicity](../../../../acts/ergodicity.md), combining the fixed kernel
  with mixing.
- Uses: [Mixing Property](../mixing-property.md) and
  [Fixed Invariant State](../fixed-invariant-state.md).
- Shows that: the earned ergodicity act yields a property carrier used in
  entropy/spectral convergence arguments.
---

# Evaluation Context

A structure `EvalCtx = (Frame, empty)` induced by the acts
[`SeedEvaluationContext`](../seed-evaluation-context.md) and
[`BuildEvaluationContext`](../build-evaluation-context.md).

- `Frame` — evaluation frames with a single distinguished hole.
- `empty ∈ Frame` — neutral frame.

The associated operators plug/compose are defined separately.

## Proof

Sketch: from `SeedEvaluationContext` we obtain `empty ∈ Frame`; from
`BuildEvaluationContext` we generate frames. Define `Frame` as the closure of
those acts; show `empty` is neutral under composition (see compose operator)
and that plugging preserves earned recognitions/trace context.

# Fiber Category

## Definition
- Carrier: for each context in the [Index Base](../index-base.md), the category whose objects are recognised terms, types, and judgements established by [ProfileRecognitions](../profile-recognitions.md).
- Morphisms: judgemental morphisms between recognised objects that respect the profile’s typing and entailment rules.
- Structure: inherits identities and composition from the judgemental data supplied by [JudgeProfile](../judge-profile.md).

## Invariants
- Every fibre is cartesian closed to the extent earned by the profile recognitions; morphisms preserve typing and recognised equalities.
- Reindexing along context morphisms (defined separately) acts functorially between fibres, preserving identities and composition.

## Proof
- Derived from: [JudgeProfile](../judge-profile.md), which equips each context with recognised terms, types, and judgements.
- Shows that: the judgemental data of the profile forms a category in each context; identities and composition follow from the profile’s typing and entailment closure, yielding the fibre categories used in indexed semantics.

# Hypertensor Dynamics Endomorphism

The **Hypertensor Dynamics Endomorphism** is a local endomorphism of the
relational quasicrystalline hypertensor sheaf induced by an earned act in the
flow/measurement/tower stacks.

## Definition
- For each open set `U`, acts on sections over `U` by modifying only the
  fibres/towers determined by the act’s footprint.
- Commutes with restriction: for inclusion `V ⊂ U`,
  `res_{V,U} ∘ F(U) = F(V) ∘ res_{V,U}`.
- Built from flow/nucleus and measurement acts over the
  [Hypertensor Sheaf](../hypertensor-sheaf.md).

## Invariants
- Locality: only touches fibres/towers in the act’s footprint.
- Closure: endomorphisms form a monoid under composition (include identity).
- Compatibility: respects the sheaf structure (restriction/gluing).

## Proof
Derived from flow/nucleus/measurement acts applied to the hypertensor sheaf,
ensuring locality and restriction-compatibility.
---

# Relational Quasicrystalline Hypertensor Sheaf

The **Relational Quasicrystalline Hypertensor Sheaf** packages the earned
window, fiber, and tower carriers into a sheaf with sections, restriction, and
gluing over the relational site.

## Definition
- Carriers:
  - `Window`: earned lattice windows from trace decomposition and fixed-point
    substitution (see [Hypertensor Window](../hypertensor-window.md)).
  - `Fiber`: traced-context carriers over windows (see
    [Hypertensor Fiber](../hypertensor-fiber.md)).
  - `Tower`: profile towers attached to each fiber (see
    [Hypertensor Tower](../hypertensor-tower.md)).
- Sheaf: applying the sheaf acts (presheaf, sheaf condition, sheafification)
  yields `(Section, restrict, glue)` as a sheaf over the earned coverage.
- State: set `State := Section`; this is the math-level state carrier.

## Invariants
- Sheaf axioms hold (matching families glue uniquely over covers).
- Flow/nucleus commute with restrict/glue as earned by the flow/nucleus acts.
- State is unique up to earned equivalence.

## Proof
Derived from the sheaf acts applied to the earned window/fiber/tower carriers
over the relational coverage. The flow/nucleus commutation acts ensure stability
under dynamics. State is defined as the section carrier of this sheaf.
---

# Hypertensor Window Section

The **Hypertensor Window Section** is a finite section of the relational
quasicrystalline hypertensor sheaf over a finite lattice window.

## Definition
- Components:
  - a finite [Hypertensor Window](../hypertensor-window.md)
    recording dimension, sites, and window metadata;
  - a finite family of [Profile Towers](../profile-tower.md)
    attached to fibres whose trace/context positions lie inside the window.
- Equality: two representations `(W, {T_i})` and `(W', {T'_j})` describe the
  same section when related by sheaf restriction/gluing and nucleus-equivalence
  from the hypertensor and tower theorems.

## Invariants
- Restriction: restricting to a subwindow applies sheaf restriction on the
  window and restricts each tower to fibres over the subwindow, discarding
  towers outside.
- Gluing: compatible families over covers glue to a window section.

## Proof
Derived from **Theorem (Relational Quasicrystalline Hypertensor Sheaf)** and
the tower interaction/lifting results. These ensure that finite sections over
open charts supported on finite windows are represented by a window plus a
finite family of attached profile towers.
---

# Hypertensor Window

## Statement

There exists a single lattice window carrier

```text
Window
```

earned by trace decomposition/shuffle and fixed-point acts, subject to the
quasicrystalline constraints.

## Construction by acts

- [`decompose-trace`](../decompose-trace.md),
  [`shuffle-trace`](../shuffle-trace.md), and
  [`collapse-shuffle`](../collapse-shuffle.md) extract lattice windows from
  traces.
- [`ensure-fixed-point`](../ensure-fixed-point.md) fixes the substitution
  flow, yielding a stable window basis.
- External quasicrystal constraints from
  [`reference/quasicrystalline-relational-hypertensors`](../../../../../../../../content/text/documents/articles/notes/undated/quasicrystalline-relational-hypertensors.md)
  restrict admissible windows.

## Uniqueness

Given these acts and constraints, any earned window carrier is
nucleus-equivalent; `Window` is unique up to earned equivalence.

# Indexed Category

## Definition
- Base: [Index Base](../index-base.md) supplies contexts and their morphisms.
- Fibres: [Fiber Category](../fiber-category.md) gives a category of recognised judgements over each base context.
- Reindexing: [Reindexing](../reindexing.md) maps each base morphism to a functor between the corresponding fibres.
- Structure: assembles these into a functor `IndexBaseᵒᵖ → Cat`, yielding the indexed category of contextual judgements.

## Invariants
- Reindexing preserves fibre identities and composition, making each base morphism act functorially on fibres.
- Beck–Chevalley coherence is inherited from the functoriality of reindexing: pullbacks along composed base morphisms agree with iterated pullbacks.

## Proof
- Uses: [Index Base](../index-base.md) as the domain category, [Fiber Category](../fiber-category.md) for each fibre, and [Reindexing](../reindexing.md) to transport along base morphisms.
- Shows that: these pieces satisfy the definition of an indexed category—every context has a fibre category and every base morphism induces a functor between fibres—forming a contravariant functor into `Cat`.
---

# Judgment Calculus Soundness

Metatheory of the internal judgment calculus: preservation and progress earned
by `SoundSyntax`, `PreserveTyping`, and `ProgressTyping`.

## Components

- Preservation: if `Γ ⊢ t : A` and `t → t'` (relational evaluation), then
  `Γ ⊢ t' : A`.
- Progress: every well-typed closed term is either a value or can take a
  relational evaluation step.

## Proof

Sketch: `SoundSyntax` sets admissible derivations; `PreserveTyping` and
`ProgressTyping` acts witness preservation and progress for the earned
evaluation relation and typing judgments. Combine to obtain soundness for the
judgment calculus.
---

# Relational Logic Algebra

The **Relational Logic Algebra** is the internal logic on earned recognitions
over the recognition carrier. Externally it corresponds to a Heyting algebra;
internally it is the judgment/recognition algebra.

## Definition
- Order with bottom/top: earned by `OrderRecognition`, `GroundRecognition`,
  `CapRecognition`.
- Finite meets/joins: earned by `MeetRecognition`, `JoinRecognition`.
- Implication/negation: earned by `ImplyRecognition`, `Negate`.

## Invariants
- Forms a bounded lattice with Heyting implication and pseudocomplement.
- All operations stay within the recognition carrier.

## Proof
Derived from the listed acts, which individually earn the order, lattice, and
implication/negation operations; together they form the Heyting algebra on
recognitions.

## Notes
- Alias of the internal judgment/recognition algebra; external readers may
  recognize it as a Heyting algebra.
---

# Loop Space

Carrier of based loops (directed paths from a point to itself) with homotopy
identifications from the loop HIT.

## Definition
- Basepoint: a chosen recognition/context `x`.
- Loops: directed paths `p: x → x` modulo homotopy given by
  [LoopHIT](../loop-hit.md).
- Operations: composition, unit loop, associator/unitor homotopies.

## Invariants
- Unit: identity loop acts as unit for composition up to earned homotopy.
- Associativity: loop composition associative up to earned homotopy.
- Directedness: reversal not assumed; if reversible, provide explicit inverse.

## Proof
- Derived from: [LoopHIT](../loop-hit.md) which earns loops and their
  homotopies.
- Uses: [Directed Path](../directed-path.md) to form loops.
- Shows that: loops form a homotopy-algebraic structure supporting composition,
  unit, and associativity witnesses.
---

# Mixing Property

Structure capturing the mixing property of a flow/observation kernel: long-time
evolution makes states asymptotically independent of initial conditions.

## Definition
- Carrier: predicates on [Observation Kernel](../2026/03/04/2026-03-04-observation-kernel.md) (and its
  iterates) stating that correlations decay to zero as time progresses.
- Data: mixing rate or bounds (when earned) and the sigma-algebra/recognition
  tower over which independence is measured.

## Invariants
- Decay: correlations of observables composed with the kernel converge to the
  product of expectations.
- Stability: preserved under iteration of the observation kernel; compatible
  with the profile tower when lifted.

## Proof
- Derived from: [Mixing](../mixing.md) act, which witnesses decay of
  correlations under the kernel.
- Uses: [Observation Kernel](../2026/03/04/2026-03-04-observation-kernel.md) as the underlying dynamic.
- Shows that: the mixing act induces a well-defined property carrier for kernels
  used by entropy and spectral fixed-point arguments.

# Modal Structure

The **Modal Structure** equips the recognition state space with modal operators
and their iterated tower.

## Definition
- States: stabilised recognitions over the [Recognition Carrier](../recognition-carrier.md).
- Modal operators: `Box`, `Diamond` from [`Box Modality`](../box-modality.md)
  and [`Diamond Modality`](../diamond-modality.md); additional modalities
  (truncation/irrational/quadratic) from their acts.
- Tower: iterated modalities via [Modality Tower](../modality-tower.md).

## Invariants
- Modal operators are monotone and preserve finite meets/joins of the
  [Relational Logic Algebra](../2026/03/04/2026-03-04-logic-algebra.md).
- Adjunctions (if earned) between modal pairs (e.g., box/diamond) hold fiberwise.
- Towers iterate modalities coherently; tower levels commute with reindexing
  along contexts.

## Proof
Derived from the modal acts; operators extend the logic algebra, and the tower
act builds iterated applications. Compatibility with the algebra follows from
the earned acts.
---

# Observation Kernel

The **Observation Kernel** assigns to each fixed invariant state an
observational distribution determined by a flow–measure transition.

## Definition
- Built from [Observation Kernel](../2026/03/04/2026-03-04-observation-kernel.md) using the
  [Transition Law](../transition-law.md).
- Maps [Fixed Invariant State](../fixed-invariant-state.md) elements to
  distributions over observations after one transition step.

## Invariants
- Flow–measure property: future evolution depends only on the current state.
- Respects the invariant lattice (order/meet/join up to stabilization).
- Stochasticity: preserves total mass; positivity of transition probabilities.
- Compatibility: consistent with [Hypertensor Stochastic Evolution](../hypertensor-stochastic-evolution.md)
  when restricted to observations.

## Proof
Derived from the observation-kernel act, which attaches observational
distributions to transition-law steps and establishes lattice compatibility.

Certificate that observations suffice to witness contextual equivalence in the
full profile.

## Definition
- Carrier: witnesses produced by applying [Observe](../observe.md) through
  [Witness Observation](../witness-observation.md) to establish contextual
  equivalence.
